# CLI contract

Status: accepted, 2026-09-18. Implements the output contract in ADR 0001 and the machine state layout in ADR 0004. The desktop app, scripts and tests depend on this document; change it together with the CLI.

## Invocation

`agentx [--json] [--verbose] [--color on|off] <command> [arguments]`. The three global flags are accepted before or after the command. `--json` switches stdout to newline-delimited JSON events. `--verbose` raises the log level on stderr to debug. `--color` decides whether text output carries ANSI colour, see [Streams](#streams); any other value is a usage error. `agentx help`, `--help`, `-h` and a bare `agentx` print usage and exit 0; the usage text goes to stdout, or to stderr in JSON mode. A bare `agentx --json` is a usage error instead: a script that omits the command has made a mistake.

The CLI reads its environment once, at startup, from the variables below. Beyond them, `PATH` locates git and the command of a local MCP server, `TZ` is the time zone the dates of [fork commits](#git) are written in, the local one when it is unset, and the [handshake](#handshake) hands the environment on to the servers it starts.

| Variable | Meaning | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `AGENTX_HOME` | agentx home | `$HOME/.agentx` |
| `AGENTX_LIBRARY` | the library | `$HOME/.agents/skills` |
| `HOME` | the user's home; agent client paths derive from it | required |
| `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` | where agent clients keep user-scope configuration | `$HOME/.config` |
| `XDG_STATE_HOME` | where the vercel skills CLI keeps the lock file [`agentx adopt`](#adoption) reads | unset; the `.agents` location below is then the one that tool writes |
| `AGENTX_PLATFORM_ID` | the platform id the machine id is derived from; set it empty to declare that the machine has none | the operating system's platform id, see [Machine identity](#machine-identity) |
| `AGENTX_HOSTNAME` | the default machine label | the operating system's hostname |
| `AGENTX_INSTANCE_ID` | the `instance_id` carried by snapshots, so a script can fix it | a fresh random id per process |
| `AGENTX_HANDSHAKE_TIMEOUT` | the budget of one MCP handshake, a duration such as `500ms` | `10s` |
| `AGENTX_CHECK_INTERVAL` | how often [serve](#serve) runs the update check, which refreshes every added source, a duration such as `10m`; anything that is not a positive duration is exit code 1, read by every `agentx serve` except `serve --once`, whether or not it has anything to check | `30m` |

## Streams

Without `--json`, stdout carries aligned text and stderr carries log lines as `level: message`, with `error: <message>` and `hint: <fix>` for a failure. A `warning:` can have a second line right under it, indented by two spaces, that says what to do (the warning for a kept copy under **Placements** does); with `--json` it is still one `log` event, whose message is the two lines joined by `; `. A table pads every column to its widest cell with two spaces between columns and nothing after the last cell. A row that reports a state starts with a glyph: `✓` ok, `!` warning, `✗` failure, `•` information. A command that changes something confirms it on one line starting with `✓`; the wording is the command's own.

Text output is coloured per stream when that stream is a terminal, the `NO_COLOR` variable is not set (see no-color.org) and `TERM` is not `dumb`; `--color on` colours a pipe too, and `--color off` colours nothing. Colour is added by SGR escape sequences around the text and changes nothing else: stripping the sequences gives the exact text a pipe receives, alignment included. JSON output is never coloured, whatever `--color` says. The palette has one meaning per colour: bold for a heading or the name that starts a row, cyan for the key of a key-value row and for a command or flag name, dim for secondary detail, green for ok and enabled, yellow for a warning, a hint and disabled, bold red for a failure and an error, blue for information, magenta for a `(plugin <name>)` marker, cyan for a count such as `12 tools`.

Text agentx did not write is sanitised before it is printed: every control character becomes a space, runs of spaces become one and the leading and trailing ones are dropped. That is a skill's name and description, which the repository a source was added from supplies; the name of a directory in the library or under a client's configuration, which whoever made the directory chose; the command line, the URL and the version a configuration file or a plugin manifest declares; and whatever agentx relays of what something else said, such as the stderr of a git it ran, which carries the messages a server sent it. A control character is one of the 65 characters Unicode names as such: the C0 controls `U+0000` to `U+001F`, `DEL` at `U+007F`, and the C1 controls `U+0080` to `U+009F`, which a terminal obeys as readily as the C0 ones. Nothing above `U+00FF` is covered: a format character such as a bidirectional override or a zero-width joiner is printed as it is, being what several writing systems are spelled with and what an emoji sequence is joined with. A value carrying a newline therefore cannot break the one row per item a listing prints, a tab cannot disturb a column, a carriage return cannot overwrite the line already printed, and no escape sequence reaches the terminal, whatever `--color` says. Nothing is parsed: agentx does not recognise an escape sequence and strip it whole, since a parser that misjudged one sequence's end would let the rest through, while a rule that admits no control character at all cannot; what a sequence leaves behind once its `ESC` is a space, the `[31m` of a red, prints as the ordinary text it is.

A diff is printed rather than sanitised, being text a reader reads for its layout: `agentx skill diff` prints the lines git wrote as they are, a file's own bytes included, a byte that is not valid UTF-8 among them, with every control character but the tab turned into a space of its own. So is a byte from `0x80` to `0x9F` that is no part of a UTF-8 character, which a terminal set to 8-bit controls reads as a C1 control, `0x9B` being the one-byte form of the escape that starts a sequence; every other byte that is not valid UTF-8 is printed as it is. No escape sequence reaches the terminal, and every space and every tab of an indentation stays where it was. Its `diff` events carry the patch git wrote, and the path, as JSON strings, and every string of an event is UTF-8: a byte that is not valid UTF-8 arrives as `U+FFFD`, as it would in any other event. The `patch` of a file that is not UTF-8 text, and the `path` of a file whose name is not UTF-8, are therefore there to be shown and cannot be applied byte for byte.

A path is quoted rather than sanitised, being text a reader copies: a space where a control character was names nothing on disk and says nothing about why. A path carrying one is printed whole in double quotes, with `\a`, `\b`, `\t`, `\n`, `\v`, `\f`, `\r`, `\"` and `\\` for the bytes that have a letter of their own and `\NNN` in octal for the rest, which is what git prints for such a path under `core.quotePath` and what a reader already knows from `git status`. A path carrying no control character is printed as it is, non-ASCII included.

Every line agentx puts on stderr (`warning:` and the line under it, `hint:`, `error:` and the hint under it, `info:`, `debug:`) is sanitised as it is written, whoever composed it: a message is plain text by then, agentx painting only the word that starts it, so one rule at the one exit covers every command that relays what something else said. A value that goes into a table cell on stdout is sanitised where the cell is built instead, a cell being free to carry the sequences agentx painted it with. Either way it happens before anything is painted, so the rule above still holds: stripping the SGR sequences agentx adds gives the exact text a pipe receives. JSON output is not sanitised, its values being escaped already, so a consumer reads what the source wrote: a `source_skill` event carries the name and the description as the `SKILL.md` frontmatter holds them, byte for byte, which is what lets whoever reads the events show the state of the files on this machine.

With `--json`, stdout carries only events, one JSON object per line, and nothing else. Stderr carries log events in the same envelope. A consumer reads the exit code before trusting either stream.

## Event envelope

Every event is one JSON object with at least:

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `type` | string | the event type |
| `schema_version` | integer | the output schema version, currently `1` |

`schema_version` changes when an event changes incompatibly. A consumer refuses a CLI whose schema version it does not know. A field added to an event, and a value added to a field this document says will gain more, are not such a change and leave the version where it is: a consumer ignores a field it does not know, and shows such a value it does not know as it is written.

## Event types

Every run emits exactly one `result` as its last stdout event, after any `error`.

`version`, emitted by `agentx version`:

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `cli_version` | string | the CLI version, `dev` for an unreleased build |

`settings`, emitted by every `agentx config` command and, as its first event, by `agentx import`:

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `settings` | object | the machine settings as they now are, in the shape of the settings file below, with `label` filled in from the hostname when it is not set |

`machine`, emitted by every `agentx machine` command:

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | string | the machine id, 32 lowercase hex characters |
| `label` | string | the machine label |
| `derivation` | string | `platform` when the id is derived from the platform id, `random` when it comes from `machine.json` |

`source`, emitted by every `agentx source` command, one per source (see [Sources](#sources)), the account remote included (see [Account remote](#account-remote)), by `agentx skill add` for the source it installs from, and by `agentx skill add` with no source for the upstream source of a skill it installs from the account remote, when it adds one (see **Installing from the account remote** under [Account remote](#account-remote)):

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | string | the source id, 16 lowercase hex characters |
| `url` | string | the canonical URL |
| `alias` | string | a second URL mapped onto the canonical one; absent until aliases exist |
| `account` | boolean | `true` for the account remote; absent otherwise |
| `pin` | string | the ref the source is pinned to; absent when it follows the remote's default branch |
| `access` | string | what this machine may do at the source, as the last check found: `writable`, `read-only` or `unknown` (see **Access** under [Sources](#sources)); absent from the event of `source remove` |
| `access_checked` | string | when the access was last checked, RFC 3339 in UTC; absent when it never was |
| `access_reason` | string | why the access is not `writable`, one line of git's or the host's answer as they wrote it, or agentx's own words; present only in the event of a run that checked the source: `source add`, `source add --account` included, `skill add` when it adds or fetches the source, `adopt` and `skill add` with no source when they add one, and `source fetch` |
| `default_branch` | string | the branch the remote's `HEAD` named when it was last looked at; shown, never used to choose a branch; absent when it is not known, and for a pinned source unless an earlier look found one |
| `subpath` | string | the directory the command's listing was scoped to; absent for the whole repository, never stored |
| `last_fetched` | string | when the source was last fetched, RFC 3339 in UTC; absent from the event of `source remove` |
| `forks` | integer | for the account remote, how many fork branches its last fetch found; absent for a shared source, from the event of `source remove`, and for an account remote this machine has not fetched |
| `commit` | string | the fetched commit; absent when the account repo holds no ref for the source, and for the account remote |
| `previous_commit` | string | what the source ref held before this fetch; absent when the fetch did not move it and when the source is new |
| `skills` | integer | how many skills the listing found; present after `add`, `fetch`, `skills` and `skill add`, absent from `list` and `remove` |

`source_skill`, emitted by `agentx source skills`, one per installable skill, after the `source` event, sorted by `subpath`:

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `source` | string | the source id |
| `subpath` | string | the skill directory from the repository root, `""` when the root itself is the skill |
| `name` | string | the frontmatter `name`, else the directory name (the repository name for the root) |
| `description` | string | the frontmatter `description`, `""` when it has none |
| `tree` | string | the tree id of the skill directory at the fetched commit |

`progress`, emitted by a command that does one thing several times, one event per step as that step ends:

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `phase` | string | what the step did, one word per kind of step the command has: `fetch` for one source of `agentx source fetch` and of `agentx skill check-updates`, for one source `agentx adopt` has to add, for the source `agentx skill publish` of a skill of a shared source fetches, and for the account remote that `agentx source add --account`, `agentx skill publish`, `agentx skill list --remote`, `agentx skill add` with no source, and `agentx skill remove --remote` fetch, its subject being the remote's URL; `publish` for one skill of `agentx skill publish`, a fork or a skill of a shared source, its subject being the skill's name, each emitted once the push answered or the skill was found to need none; `blobs`, `import` and `install` for one skill of `agentx skill add`; `place` for one skill of `agentx config enable --place-all`; `adopt` for one skill of `agentx adopt`; and `rescan` for the reading of the affected configurations that ends such a run |
| `subject` | string | what the step was about: the source's canonical URL for a fetch, the skill's library name for a step of an install; absent for a step that covers the run as a whole, such as the rescan |
| `current` | integer | how many steps have ended, this one included, so that one run counts from 1 to `total` |
| `total` | integer | how many steps the run has in all; absent when the run does not know that in advance. A run that gives up on part of its work takes the steps it will no longer run out of `total`, so a run that ends still emits a last event whose `current` is its `total` |

A step is reported when it ends rather than when it starts, so that `current` is the count a progress bar needs and a run emits one event per step however it schedules them. Steps taken in parallel end in the order the network decides, so the order of the subjects is not fixed and is not the order the run reports its results in; each event says on its own what it covers, which is what a consumer reads. The event carries no outcome: a step that failed is a `log` warning, and the `error` and `result` events name what failed.

`library_skill`, emitted by `agentx skill list`, one per directory of the library sorted by name; by `agentx skill add`, one per skill it installed, from a source or from the account remote; by `agentx skill new`, one for the skill it created; by `agentx skill fork`, one for the fork it made and, for a fork made beside a skill the library holds, one for that skill after it; by `agentx skill publish`, one for each skill whose edits it recorded, sorted by name, after every `publish` event of the run, and, for a skill of a shared source, one for the skill once its source holds what it published, none when it had no edits to publish; by `agentx skill place`, one for the skill it placed; by `agentx skill remove --from <configuration>`, one for the skill whose placements it took away, and none at all when the skill left the library, as it does with `--from universal`; by `agentx skill update`, one per skill it updated, sorted by name, and one for each fork that only its account step moved (see **Updating a fork** under [Skills](#skills)), and with `--abort` one for the skill whose merge it gave up; and by `agentx config enable --place-all`, one per directory of the library sorted by name. Everywhere but `skill list` and `skill update` its `placements` are those the rescan found in the configurations that command covered, so a universal client the command did not cover, a disabled one or one `--to` left out, is not among them; `universal` names every universal client, whichever command emits the event. A [snapshot](#snapshot) carries the same object, without `type` and `schema_version`, for every skill of the library under `library`, so the desktop app and a script are told the same thing about a skill:

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` | string | the library directory name, which is also the name of its branch in the account repo |
| `kind` | string | `managed` for a skill with a branch in the account repo, an import branch of a shared source's skill or a branch of the fork namespace holding one of your own, and `unmanaged` for one with neither. A skill of a shared source and one of your own are both `managed`; `source` tells them apart, and `upstream` tells a forked skill (see **Forks** under [Skills](#skills)) |
| `fork_id` | string | the permanent id of one of your own skills, the `Agentx-Fork-ID` of the nearest commit carrying one on the first-parent chain of its branch, which is the commit `skill new` or `skill fork` wrote to create it; it is the skill's logical identity, not its root commit, and stays the same through `skill rename`, and two skills created independently, of the same upstream version or under the same name on two machines, have different ids. Absent for a skill of a shared source and an unmanaged skill, and for one of your own whose history carries none (see **Forks** under [Skills](#skills)) |
| `source` | string | where the skill is published to. For a skill of a shared source, the canonical URL of that source, from its import commit's lineage trailers. For your own skills, the ones `skill new` and `skill fork` make and the ones installed from the account remote, the canonical URL of the [account remote](#account-remote), the `url` of the settings entry marked `account`; absent for them while no account remote is set. Absent for an unmanaged skill |
| `subpath` | string | the skill's directory in that source, `""` for its root; present for a skill of a shared source alone, since one of your own is the whole of its branch `skills/<name>` on the account remote |
| `upstream` | string | where a forked skill came from, which its updates come from (see **Updating a fork** under [Skills](#skills)): the canonical URL of the source its base import commit names, which the walk of its history finds (see **Forks** under [Skills](#skills)). Present only for one of your own skills made by `skill fork` of a skill of a shared source, or of a forked skill that has an upstream, however it reached this machine, and absent when its history does not say which import is its base |
| `upstream_subpath` | string | the skill's directory in the upstream, `""` for its root; absent with `upstream` |
| `upstream_commit` | string | the upstream commit of the version the skill is based on, the source's for a skill of a shared source and the upstream's for a forked one: the last commit there that touched the skill's directory when it was read; absent for a skill based on no such version, as one `skill new` made is |
| `base_hash` | string | the content hash of that base version, from the import commit's trailer; absent with `upstream_commit` |
| `content_hash` | string | the content hash of what the library directory holds now |
| `state` | string | `current` when the library directory holds exactly its base version, `modified` when it was edited since, by any tool: for every managed skill, of a shared source as much as one of your own, `modified` means edits not yet published, since a publish makes what it published the base version (see **Publishing a skill of a shared source** under [Skills](#skills)); present for a skill of a shared source and for one of your own, a fork, whose library entry leads into its worktree, see the end of this entry, and absent for every other skill. The two are told apart as git tells a work tree from a commit: git runs with the account repo as its git directory, the library directory as its work tree and a throwaway index loaded from the base version, `read-tree <import commit>:<upstream directory>`, then `add -A` and `write-tree`, and the skill is `modified` when the tree git writes is not the base version's, so the rules of **Files that do not count** under [Skills](#skills) apply: a file git ignores never makes a skill `modified`, and a file the base version holds always counts. Git runs only when a fast path cannot tell: the directory is read in process as git would record it, every entry with `lstat` and never followed, a regular file as a blob of mode `100644`, or `100755` when its owner may execute it, a symlink as a blob of mode `120000` holding its target, a directory as a tree and an empty one left out, in git's order and with git's object headers; when its tree id, wrapped under the upstream's own directory name the way the [import commit](#skills) wraps it, is the tree of the import commit the branch points at, which the one `for-each-ref` that reads the lineage reads as well, the skill is `current` and no git runs. While `ignore_system_files` is on and the directory holds no `.gitignore`, the same holds of the tree id with every entry the list names left out. A directory holding something git cannot record, a `.git` component in any case or anything that is neither a file, a directory nor a symlink, and one that cannot be read whole, is `modified`. A mode and a link are content: a file made executable, a file replaced by a symlink to the same bytes and a symlink leading out of the skill are edits, which `content_hash` does not see, since it reads neither modes nor links out of the skill; `content_hash` and `base_hash` stay the names of the two versions and decide nothing here. It is what the base version can tell on its own: whether an upstream moved is in `candidate`, as the last update check found it, and every other way the skill has drifted is in `drift`. A fork, one of your own skills, is compared with what the account remote holds of it rather than with a base version: it is `current` when its skill directory, the one its library symlink leads to in its worktree, holds what the tip of its branch records and that tip is published, and `modified` while it holds edits not yet published. Those are the files `git status` in the worktree would list, and the commits of its branch that the account remote's branch lacks as the last fetch left it, `refs/remotes/<account remote>/skills/<name>`, or, for a renamed skill not yet published under its new name, the old name's (see **Comparing a fork**): a tip other than that branch's tip that is not the newest commit of its history the branch holds, `merge-base <tip> <remote tip>`, is unpublished, so `state` and `skill diff` (see **Comparing a fork**) compare with the same commit. Edits an update or a fork recorded, a publish whose push failed recorded, or a `git commit` in the worktree made, so stay `modified` until a publish pushes them; nothing is fetched to decide it. A fork the account remote holds no branch of, or one sharing no commit with it, never published or with no account remote set, is compared with its creation commit instead, the one `skill new` or `skill fork` wrote, which carries its fork id: it is `current` until it is edited or its branch gains a commit; one whose history carries no fork id is compared with its tip alone. Whether the directory holds the tip is decided the same way and by the same rules: the fast path compares the directory's tree id, wrapped under the skill's directory name, with the tree of the tip, which the same `for-each-ref` reads, and otherwise git runs in the skill directory with the worktree as its work tree, over a throwaway index loaded from the tip's whole tree and never over the worktree's own index, adds the skill directory alone and writes the tree, and the fork is `current` when that tree is the tip's. So a `.gitignore` at the worktree's root counts as it counts for `git status`, and an entry a commit made with git put beside the skill directory, which the fast path never matches, stays as the tip holds it and changes nothing. A Git repository nested in the directory that the ignore rules do not cover is `modified` too. A fork whose library entry is a directory of its own, or leads anywhere but into the fork's worktree, has neither `state` nor `drift`: that directory is not the fork's checkout |
| `drift` | array of strings | the drift states the skill is in beside its `state`, sorted; absent when it is in none, and always for an unmanaged skill and for a fork without a `state`. `displaced` and `missing` say what the placements are, read at each configuration's own place, `<the client's own skills directory>/<name>`, and nowhere else, for every enabled configuration whose client does not read the library: `displaced` when one holds a real directory where agentx keeps a symlink, which is what an installer that copies over links leaves, or the symlink naming the library directory where `copy_mode` records a copy; `missing` when one holds nothing at all, whether the skill was never placed there, `--to` having left it out, or its placement went, by `--from` or by hand. A client that sees the skill through another client's skills directory, as Cursor reads Claude Code's, is still missing it. A disabled configuration and a universal client are never either: the one is a configuration the user chose not to place into, and the other's placement is the library entry itself, so a link or copy a universal client may still hold in its own skills directory, as Cursor may hold `~/.cursor/skills/<name>` once Claude Code's skills directory is a symlink to the library, is neither read nor put back. Nor is a real directory that is the library directory itself, which a library entry made a symlink to a configuration's own place leaves there: the client reads the library's own directory, and nothing is displaced. A symlink of the user's to anywhere else takes the place, and so does a copy whose content is not the library's, which is still the copy (see **Placements** under [Skills](#skills)), and anything else at the path: the skill is not missing there, and nothing agentx keeps was displaced. Both are information: they never fail a command and change no exit code, and agentx never puts back what they name on its own: `agentx skill place` puts them back (see **Putting placements back** under [Skills](#skills)), and `skill add` and `config enable --place-all` put back those at the configurations they place into, by their rules for what is already at the path (see **Placements** under [Skills](#skills)), which leave a directory whose content differs from the library's where it is. The third is `source removed`, when the canonical URL its `Agentx-Source` trailer names is the `url` of no source entry in the [machine settings](#machine-settings), because the source was removed with `source remove` or was never added on this machine. It is derived on every read from the lineage and the settings and never written anywhere, and a source whose entry is there and whose ref the account repo does not hold is not removed: that is a source not fetched, which is what an import leaves. The coordinates stay as the lineage has them, since they are still where the skill came from and what adding the source again needs. The drift states are a list beside `state` rather than more values of it because each answers a question of its own and any of them can hold together with `current` or `modified` and with one another: a skill edited by hand whose source was then removed is `modified` and `source removed`, and one word would hide the other. The fourth is `upstream removed`, when the last [update check](#skills) found the skill's source without it, no directory at its subpath or one without a `SKILL.md`: the check records that in the marker ref `refs/agentx/upstream-removed/<name>`, which the listing reads with the lineage in the same `for-each-ref`, and which only a check writes and deletes, once it finds the skill again; a removal of the skill takes it away. It stays whatever the source does meanwhile, until a check fetches the source again, and a source removed since is not checked, so a skill can be `source removed` and `upstream removed` at once. Further drift states join this list. A fork's placements are `displaced` and `missing` as a managed skill's are, judged at the same places. A fork is not `source removed` when the source of its third-party upstream goes: the source that matters to a fork is the account remote it is published to, not the upstream it merges later versions from; nor is it ever `upstream removed`, which the update check records for a managed skill alone. An unmanaged skill has no source at all. Every drift state is read anew on every read, `upstream removed` from the marker the last check left and the others from the lineage, the settings and the filesystem; [serve](#serve) emits a `drift` event when one changes. A managed branch whose library directory is gone has no entry, and so no drift at all: a warning names it instead, see **Listing** under [Skills](#skills) |
| `candidate` | object | the update the last update check found, for a managed skill or a fork whose candidate ref `refs/agentx/candidate/<name>` holds an import commit: `upstream_commit`, the upstream commit that newer version was read at, and `content_hash`, the newer version's content hash, both from the import commit's trailers; absent when there is none, which is what a check leaves for a skill whose source holds its base version or no longer holds it at all, and when the ref names the commit the import branch already holds, which is no update (see [Mutation journal](#mutation-journal)); a fork's is judged against its base version, the import commit its history names, and is absent when the ref names that base, whatever the fork's own commits hold. It is read in the `for-each-ref` that reads the lineage, and stays until a check finds otherwise, whatever the source holds by now, or until `agentx skill update` applies it, which deletes it (see **Updating** under [Skills](#skills)): a check that could not reach the source, and a source removed since, leave it as it was. Inside `candidate` the two fields name the candidate's version; see [Content hash](#content-hash) |
| `pending_merge` | boolean | `true` while the skill has a pending merge: a Git merge in progress in the Git worktree `<agentx home>/merges/<name>`, which an update left when the edits of the library directory and the update conflicted (see **Updating an edited skill** under [Skills](#skills)). It is read from that directory, with no git process, and is absent when there is none. It stays until `skill update` applies the merge once it is resolved, or `skill update --abort` gives it up: a check that finds a newer version moves the `candidate` and leaves the worktree as it is |
| `placements` | array | one object per way a configuration sees the skill, sorted by `configuration` then `path`: `configuration`, `path`, `mode` (`symlink`, `copy`, or `library` for a client that reads the library directly) and `kind` (what is on disk now, the `kind` of the occurrence in a snapshot, or `library` for the library entry itself). The library entry is `library` for both however the client reaches it: through the library directory, through a skills directory that leads to it through a symlink, or at the real directory a library entry that is itself a symlink leads to |
| `universal` | array of strings | the id of every universal client detected, by the rescan of the command that emits the event or by the scan a snapshot comes from, a configuration whose client reads the library directly, `reads_library` in a snapshot, sorted by id: each one sees the skill through the library entry whatever its enabled state and whatever `--to` named, so it is listed whether or not a command placed into it; `[]` when none is detected, a client that is not installed seeing nothing |

`diff`, emitted by `agentx skill diff`, one per file of the skill that differs between the two versions it compares, sorted by path (see **Comparing with the base** under [Skills](#skills)); with `--update` the two versions are the base version and the update candidate, and for a fork they are its last published version, under its old name for a renamed skill not yet published under the new one, or its creation commit when the account remote holds no branch of it, or the commit `--commit` names, and its skill directory (see **Comparing a fork**):

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` | string | the skill's library name |
| `path` | string | the file's path relative to the skill's directory, with `/` as the separator, as the tree holds it; never inside the upstream's directory name the import tree wraps the skill in. A byte of the name that is not valid UTF-8 is `U+FFFD`, see [Streams](#streams) |
| `status` | string | what the library did to the file against the base, what a fork's skill directory did to it against the commit, or with `--update` what the update does to it: `added`, `modified` or `deleted`; a change of mode or of type, a file turned into a symlink or back, is `modified` |
| `patch` | string | the unified diff git wrote for the file, from its `diff --git a/<path> b/<path>` line to its last line, each line ending in a newline; a change of type is the two diffs git writes for it, the old content deleted and the new one added. A byte that is not valid UTF-8 is `U+FFFD`, so the patch of a file that is not UTF-8 text is for display, see [Streams](#streams) |

`history`, emitted by `agentx skill history`, one per commit of the fork's branch, newest first in the order `git log` lists them (see **History** under [Skills](#skills)):

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` | string | the fork's name |
| `commit` | string | the commit |
| `parents` | array | its parents' commits, the first parent first; empty for a root commit |
| `time` | string | its author time, RFC 3339 with the author's own offset; an import commit's is always `2000-01-01T00:00:00+00:00`, the fixed date agentx writes every import commit with so that a version has the same commit on every machine, as is that of a commit made with git in an environment that sets the same date |
| `author` | string | its author, `<name> <<email>>` |
| `subject` | string | the first line of its message |
| `message` | string | its whole message, trailers included, without the final newline |
| `machine` | string | its `Agentx-Machine` trailer, the machine id of the machine agentx wrote it on; absent for a commit without one |
| `import` | bool | whether it is an upstream import commit: no parent, and the import trailers of [Skills](#skills) |
| `foreign` | bool | whether it was made with git directly rather than by agentx: not an import and no `Agentx-Machine` trailer |
| `files` | array | one object per file it changes against its first parent, every file for a root commit, `path` relative to the skill's directory and `status` (`added`, `modified` or `deleted`, in the words of a `diff` event), in git's order; an entry beside the skill's directory, such as a `README.md` committed with git at the worktree's root, starts with `../` |

`fork_note`, emitted by `agentx skill fork` of a skill a plugin provides, one per configuration that has the plugin and gets the fork and one per universal client that does not have the plugin, sorted by configuration, after the fork's `library_skill` (see **Forking a skill** under [Skills](#skills)):

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` | string | the fork's name |
| `plugin` | string | the plugin whose skill was forked, whose own copy stays where it is |
| `configuration` | string | the configuration the note is about |
| `note` | string | how its client treats the fork beside the plugin's copy, from the client registry: Claude Code, `the fork owns the bare name and the plugin's copy stays reachable under the plugin's prefix, so the model sees both`; Codex, `both load, the plugin's copy under the plugin's namespace`; Gemini CLI, `the fork replaces the plugin's copy`; Cursor and any client the registry knows nothing of, `which of the two copies wins is not documented`; a universal client without the plugin, `it reads the library, so it sees <name> whether or not it has <plugin>` |

`update_available`, emitted by `agentx skill check-updates`, one per skill of a shared source or forked skill the check could check that holds an update candidate once it is done, and one per skill of yours whose branch on the account remote holds commits another machine published, sorted by name, and by [serve](#serve) after every update check it runs (see **Checking for updates** under [Skills](#skills)). An update an earlier check pinned, which is still the newest the source holds, is announced again, so a check lists every update available among the skills it could check. One it could not check is not announced on that check, while its candidate stays, as the `candidate` of its `library_skill` shows: a skill whose source was unreachable or removed, and one whose newer version the check could not read or refuses to import, which a warning names; serve names the skills of a source it could not fetch in a warning only when that failure starts or its cause changes, and in a `debug` line on the checks in between (see [Serve](#serve)):

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `instance_id` | string | this serve process's instance id; present only when serve emits the event |
| `name` | string | the skill's library name |
| `kind` | string | `managed`, as every skill an update applies to is |
| `source` | string | where the skill is published to, as its `library_skill` has it: the canonical URL of its shared source, or for one of your own the canonical URL of the account remote, absent while none is set. For an update another machine published, the canonical URL of the account remote |
| `subpath` | string | the skill's directory in that source, `""` for its root; present for a skill of a shared source alone |
| `upstream` | string | for a forked skill, which is compared by its base version, the canonical URL of the upstream that version came from and the newer one comes from; absent for every other update |
| `upstream_subpath` | string | the skill's directory in the upstream, `""` for its root; absent with `upstream` |
| `base_hash` | string | the content hash of the base version, a forked skill's being the import commit its history names |
| `upstream_commit` | string | the upstream commit of the base version, the source's or the upstream's |
| `candidate` | string | the id of the candidate, the import commit of the newer version, which `refs/agentx/candidate/<name>` holds: the commit an install of that version would write; for an update another machine published, the commit the account remote's branch of the skill holds, which no ref of the check records |
| `candidate_hash` | string | the content hash of the newer version |
| `candidate_upstream_commit` | string | the upstream commit of the newer version |
| `files` | array | one object per file the update changes, `path` relative to the skill's directory and `status` (`added`, `modified` or `deleted`, in the words of a `diff` event), sorted by path; the diffs themselves are what `agentx skill diff <name> --update` shows |
| `upstream_name` | string | the name an install of the newer version would give the skill, its frontmatter `name` or else the upstream's directory name, present only when it is not the library name. The skill keeps its library name, branch and placements through an update, and the `SKILL.md` is never rewritten |

An update another machine published to one of your own skills, see **Checking for updates**, carries `base_hash`, `upstream_commit`, `candidate_hash` and `candidate_upstream_commit` empty and no `subpath`, `upstream` or `upstream_name`: that is what tells it from a newer upstream version. Its `files` are what the account remote's branch changed since the last commit it shares with the branch here.

`conflict`, emitted by `agentx skill update`, one per skill whose edits conflicted with its update or whose pending merge still has files to resolve, sorted by name, after the `library_skill` events of the skills it updated (see **Updating an edited skill** under [Skills](#skills)), and by `agentx skill update` (its account step), one per fork whose merge with the account remote conflicted, after that fork's `pull` event (see **The account step** under [Account remote](#account-remote)). It names the blobs of the versions of every file that conflicts, which a front end feeds to a diff tool to show the file, without reading the account repo:

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` | string | the skill's library name |
| `kind` | string | `managed`, as every skill an update merges into is |
| `base` | string | the base of the merge: the import commit the skill's branch points at, or a fork's base version, the import commit its history names; for a fork's completed merge merged again with commits made while it was pending, the tip that merge started from (see **A fork's merge pending**); for a fork's merge with the account remote, the merge base git finds of the two tips |
| `mine` | string | the commit of the library directory as git records it, the files git ignores in it aside, committed on `base`, or a fork's tip: the `HEAD` of the pending merge's worktree |
| `theirs` | string | the update candidate, the `MERGE_HEAD` of the worktree, or the candidate when a merge pending has none (see **A merge pending**); for a fork's completed merge merged again, that merge; for a fork's merge with the account remote, the remote tip |
| `files` | array | one object per file that conflicts, as `merge-tree` lists the conflicted files, which are the unmerged paths of the pending merge's worktree, or for a merge already pending as `git ls-files -u` lists those still unmerged, sorted by `path`: `path`, relative to the skill's directory as in a `diff` event; and `base`, `mine` and `theirs`, the blob of each stage git lists for that path, 1, 2 and 3, or `null` for a version with no file there, as an add on both sides has no `base`, a file one side deleted has no `mine` or no `theirs`, and each path of a file moved to two places has the one side that moved it there. A path that is not valid UTF-8 has `U+FFFD` for each byte it cannot carry, see [Streams](#streams) |

`export`, emitted by `agentx export`, one per run, naming the document it wrote (see [Export and import](#export-and-import)):

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | the file the document was written to, as the argument named it |
| `skills` | integer | how many lineage records it holds |

`import_skill`, emitted by `agentx import`, one per lineage record of the document it read, in the order the document holds them, which is by name. It carries the record as the document holds it, and what this machine's account repo holds under that name:

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` | string | the skill's name, which is the last component of its branch |
| `kind` | string | `managed`, as every skill with a branch is |
| `commit` | string | the commit the exporting machine's branch pointed at |
| `source` | string | where the skill is published to, as its `library_skill` has it: the canonical URL of its shared source, or for one of your own the canonical URL of the account remote; absent for one of your own when the exporting machine had no account remote set |
| `subpath` | string | the skill's directory in that source, `""` for its root; present for a skill of a shared source alone |
| `upstream` | string | where a forked skill came from, as its `library_skill` has it; absent for every other skill |
| `upstream_subpath` | string | the skill's directory in the upstream, `""` for its root; absent with `upstream` |
| `upstream_commit` | string | the commit the version the skill is based on was read from, the source's or the upstream's; absent for a skill based on no such version |
| `base_hash` | string | the content hash of the base version the branch records; absent with `upstream_commit` |
| `placed` | boolean | whether the exporting machine had a placement of the skill |
| `state` | string | `present` when this machine's account repo holds that name, in the namespace the record belongs in, at that commit; `missing` when it holds no branch of that name; `different` when it holds one otherwise, at another commit or in the other namespace. A record belongs under `refs/heads/skills/` when it is one of your own skills, its `source` being the account remote of the document's settings or absent, and under `refs/heads/managed/` otherwise. One of your own the repo holds no branch of is compared with the remote-tracking branch `refs/remotes/src-<id>/skills/<name>` the last fetch of the [account remote](#account-remote) left, so a machine that set the remote holds every skill published there |
| `local_commit` | string | the commit this machine holds it at, the account remote's tip when only the account remote holds it; absent when `state` is `missing` |

`pull`, emitted by `agentx skill update`, one for each fork whose account step took in something, conflicted or was refused (see **The account step** under [Account remote](#account-remote)):

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` | string | the fork's name |
| `outcome` | string | `fast-forward` when the branch moved to the remote tip, `merged` when the two tips were merged, `conflict` when the merge is pending, and `refused` when the fork took nothing in, which the `error` names |
| `commit` | string | the fork's new tip, for `fast-forward` and `merged` |
| `base` | string | the import commit the merge records in `Agentx-Base`, for `merged`; absent for a skill with no upstream, as one `skill new` made has none |

`publish`, emitted by `agentx skill publish`, one per skill it covers, in name order, after that skill's `progress` event (see **Publishing** and **Publishing a skill of a shared source** under [Skills](#skills)):

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` | string | the skill's name |
| `outcome` | string | `pushed`, its edits recorded first when it had some; `up to date` when the account remote held the skill's tip already and it had no edits to record, or, for a skill of a shared source, when it holds no edits or the source already holds what its directory holds; `behind` when the remote's branch holds the skill's tip and commits another machine published after it, and the skill holds no edits, so it has nothing to publish; `moved` when the remote's branch holds commits another machine published that the skill lacks while the skill holds commits or edits of its own, found by the publish or by the push, or, for a skill of a shared source, the source changed the skill since it was installed or moved its branch since the fetch, which the `error` names; `declined` when the host of the account remote, or of the shared source, refused the push, a hook, a protected branch or a rule of its own, which the `error` names with the host's reason; `denied` when the account remote, or the shared source, did not let this machine push, its credentials refused or none available; and `refused` for every other skill that was not published, which the `error` names |
| `commit` | string | what the account remote's branch holds now, for `pushed`, `up to date` and `behind`; for a skill of a shared source, what its branch holds now, once a publish pushed it, absent when the skill had no edits to publish |
| `source` | string | the URL of the source the skill is published to: the account remote's, or for a skill of a shared source that source's |
| `branch` | string | for a skill of a shared source this publish pushed, the branch of the source it pushed to; absent otherwise |

`installable_skill`, emitted by `agentx skill list --remote`, one per skill of yours the account remote holds that the account repo has no branch of, sorted by name, after the `library_skill` events (see **Installing from the account remote** under [Account remote](#account-remote)):

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` | string | the skill's name, the remote branch's `skills/<name>` |
| `fork_id` | string | the skill's permanent id, read from the remote tip's history; a branch whose history records none is not listed |
| `commit` | string | the remote tip, as the fetch found it |
| `source` | string | the canonical URL of the account remote, which the skill is published to |
| `upstream` | string | the canonical URL of the upstream a forked skill's base version came from; absent for a skill with no upstream, as one `skill new` made has none, and when its history does not say |
| `upstream_subpath` | string | the directory in the upstream, `""` for its root; absent with `upstream` |
| `upstream_commit` | string | the upstream commit of the base version; absent with `upstream` |
| `base_hash` | string | the content hash of the base version; absent with `upstream` |

`error`, emitted when a command fails:

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `code` | string | the string code of the exit code table below |
| `message` | string | what went wrong, naming the path or command involved |
| `hint` | string | how to fix it; absent when there is nothing to suggest |

`result`, the terminating event:

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `ok` | boolean | whether the command succeeded; the exit code is 0 exactly when it is `true` |
| `summary` | string | one line about the outcome; on failure the error message; absent when empty |

`log`, written to stderr:

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `level` | string | the log level: `warn`, `info` for a note that asks for nothing, such as a failure warned about earlier that has ended, or `debug`, and it will gain more, a level a consumer does not know being shown as it is written; `debug` lines appear only with `--verbose` |
| `message` | string | the log line; for a warning with a second line, the two lines joined by `; ` |

`snapshot`, emitted by `agentx scan`, is described under [Snapshot](#snapshot).

`configuration`, `mcp_server`, `plugin` and `skill` are node types carried inside `snapshot` and no command emits them as events of their own; the `skill` node is one content version found anywhere on the machine with its occurrences, and no event carries it. The entries of a snapshot's `library` are not nodes: each is a `library_skill` without its envelope. `doctor` is described under [Doctor](#doctor), `adoption` under [Adoption](#adoption), and `refresh_complete`, `search`, `drift` and `reconcile` under [Serve](#serve). `update_available` and `conflict` are described above; `update_available` is emitted by [serve](#serve) too, and `conflict` by `agentx skill update`. `operation` and `fleet` are reserved and never emitted.

## Exit codes

| Exit | Code | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | `ok` | success |
| 1 | `usage` | a usage error: unknown command, bad flag, missing argument, a required environment variable not set or an environment variable not valid |
| 2 | `git` | git is missing or older than 2.40 |
| 3 | `source` | a source or the account remote is unreachable or authentication failed, the account remote or a shared source did not let this machine push, or the account remote refused to delete a fork's branch |
| 4 | `pending_merge` | a pending merge blocks the action, or was left by it |
| 5 | `not_found` | a skill, source or configuration was not found |
| 6 | `refused` | a collision or an unmet precondition, a push the remote's host declined and a publish over a source that changed the skill among them |
| 7 | `locked` | another agentx command holds the lock |
| 8 | `account_repo` | the account repo is unusable: missing, corrupt, or a repository format this git cannot read |
| 9 | `interrupted` | a stop signal ended the run before it finished, see [Stopping a run](#stopping-a-run) |
| 10 | `internal` | an internal error, including a settings file or a vercel skills lock file agentx cannot read |

In JSON mode the `error` event carries the code as its `code` field.

## Stopping a run

`SIGINT` and `SIGTERM` stop a run. The first one cancels it: every git child of the run is killed, the command unwinds through its own error path, and the run reports `interrupted` with exit code 9, an `error` event and the terminating `result` like any other failure, rather than dying silently where it stood. That answer stands in for whatever the command was about to report, since a run whose git was killed reports how it died rather than what was wrong, so the hint sends the reader to `agentx doctor`, which names every kind of state a stop can leave (an unfinished [mutation journal](#mutation-journal), a remote no source names, the staging refs of an install), each with its own repair. A second signal of either kind restores that signal's default and raises it again, so a run that does not stop is killed exactly as it was before there was a handler; that is the crash the [mutation journal](#mutation-journal) is built for, and nothing about the graceful stop replaces it. A signal the process was started with ignored is left ignored, so a job a shell runs in the background, which inherits `SIGINT` ignored, is not stopped by a Ctrl-C meant for the job in the foreground.

agentx never cuts a mutation short itself. The ref steps of a mutation journal, and of the recovery a later command runs before its own work, run on a context the cancellation is taken off: by the time they reach git the journal that describes them is on disk, so giving up would leave a half-applied journal for the next command and save nothing. Everything else the run does keeps the cancellation, so a stop before the journal exists gives up at once and leaves nothing behind. An install that had already written its import commits also takes its staging refs away on that same context, and so does an update check that had written its candidates, so a stop does not leave the refs [Doctor](#doctor) reports as `staged_imports`.

A signal sent to the whole process group is the one case that cannot cover, and it is not meant to. A terminal's Ctrl-C signals the whole foreground process group, a supervisor may signal the group rather than the process it started, and the desktop app cancels a run by killing the process group it started agentx in ([ADR 0001](../adr/0001-go-cli-owns-machine-and-account-tauri-renders.md)). In each of these the git of a ref step receives the signal directly and dies whatever context agentx ran it on. Nothing is lost by that. The ref updates of each phase of a journal are one `update-ref` transaction, which commits or does not, the journal stays on disk, the next command that takes the lock finishes it, and the run still reports the stop with exit code 9. It is the crash boundary the journal is built for, reached by a different route. An unattended git, every git of the serve child and the access check of any command (see **Unattended git** under [Git](#git)), is the exception: it runs in a session of its own, outside the group, and the signal does not reach it. While agentx runs, git ends when agentx, having caught the signal, cancels the run, or, for an access check, when its 30 seconds are up; either way agentx stops git's whole process group, so the ssh or remote helper git started ends with it rather than waiting on a host that does not answer. A kill of agentx's process group, the way the desktop app cancels a run, kills agentx and not git, and with agentx gone nothing holds git to 30 seconds: on Linux the kernel kills git when agentx dies, and the ssh or remote helper it started ends once it finds git gone, or gives up on its own; on macOS git and its transport run on until they finish or give up on their own, which against a host that does not answer can take minutes, and an access check's throwaway repository stays in the temporary directory. A ref step an unattended git runs is still one transaction, and a fetch that outlives the run writes only what a fetch writes outside the lock (see [Git](#git)). A signal sent to the agentx process alone, such as one from a supervisor that signals only the process it started, is the case the paragraph above covers whole.

A run that was stopped but still finished everything it was asked to do exits 0 with its ordinary output: the signal arrived after the last step that could fail and changed nothing, and a run that did the work reports that it did. `agentx serve` ends on a cancelled context by design and exits 0 as it always did.

## Agentx home

Everything the CLI keeps on the machine lives under agentx home. Durable entries survive a reinstall when the directory is retained; transient entries coordinate running commands; derived entries are rebuilt from what the machine already has.

```
~/.agentx/
  account.git/        durable   bare account repo: skills/<name> forks, managed/<name> import branches,
                                refs/agentx/candidate/<name> update candidates,
                                refs/agentx/upstream-removed/<name> the source commit an update check
                                found without the skill (see Skills), refs/agentx/sources/<id> the last
                                fetched state of each
                                source, refs/agentx/fetching/<run>/<id> where one fetch of that source
                                stages until it is whole (see Sources), refs/agentx/importing/<run>/<n>
                                where the import commits of one skill add wait until its journal points
                                the branches at them (see Skills), refs/remotes/src-<id>/skills/<name>
                                the fork branches the last fetch of the account remote, the source
                                with that id, found there (see Account remote)
  worktrees/<name>/   durable   a placed fork: a linked worktree of skills/<name>, locked with the reason
                                agentx fork, whose one entry is the skill's directory; the library holds a
                                symlink to that directory, and the worktree's .git file sits outside it
  merges/<name>/      durable   a pending merge: a locked linked worktree of the account repo holding a
                                Git merge in progress (see Skills), never read by agents
  settings.json       durable   machine settings
  mutations/<id>.json durable   local mutation journal, kept until completion or recovery; the content it
                                stages waits in mutations/<id>.staged next to it, and a library directory,
                                placement or pending merge's worktree it stages or retains waits in a
                                hidden directory beside that live path (see Mutation journal)
  sync.json           durable   fleet upload protocol metadata; reserved, nothing writes it today
  ops/<ulid>.json     durable   operation records; reserved, nothing writes them today
  version             transient mutation counter, rewritten last by every mutating command
  lock                transient advisory lock: shared for local scans, exclusive for mutations and recovery
  serve.lock          transient advisory lock held by the one serve child of this home for its lifetime
  maintained          derived   when the serve child last maintained the account repo, as RFC 3339 (see Serve);
                                a missing one only makes the next serve child maintain it
  machine.json        derived   the machine id, only where no platform identity exists
  handshakes.json     derived   last MCP signature per server
```

No agent client reads anything in agentx home except the fork worktrees, through symlinks from the library, and none of them sees a `.git` entry: a fork's skill directory is its worktree's one subdirectory. There is no logs directory: logs go to stderr.

## Machine settings

`settings.json` in agentx home holds what only this machine decides. It is read whole and written by temp file, fsync and rename while the lock is held. A missing file means defaults. An unreadable file is exit code 10 with a hint naming the path. `schema_version` stays `1` while keys are only added, since a reader ignores a key it does not know; a file whose `schema_version` is greater than the one this agentx reads was written by a later agentx and is exit code 10 too, wherever the settings are read, with a hint to upgrade agentx rather than to delete the file. Running an agentx that predates a key on a file that holds it is not supported: an agentx that does not know a key ignores it, so one that predates the account flag reads the account remote as a shared source.

`agentx config list` prints every setting, `agentx config get <key>` one, and `agentx config set <key> <value>` changes `label`, `accept_operations` or `ignore_system_files`; the booleans take `true` or `false`. A label is one non-empty line of at most 256 bytes carrying no control character, which is the rule every route to it is held to: `config set label`, `machine rename` and the label an import restores. An unknown key, a key that `config set` cannot change, or an invalid value is exit code 1 with a hint listing the keys. Without `--json`, `list` prints a `key  value` table, showing an empty value as `(none)`, `get` prints the bare value, and `set`, `enable` and `disable` print one confirmation line: `✓ <key> is now <value>`, `✓ <id> is now enabled` or `✓ <id> is now disabled`.

`agentx config disable <id>` adds a configuration id to `disabled_configurations` and `agentx config enable <id>` removes it; both are mutations, both are no-ops when the list already has the wanted state, and both emit the `settings` event. The id must name a detected configuration (see [Snapshot](#snapshot)), else exit code 5 with a hint listing the detected ids. A detected configuration is enabled unless it is listed, so every configuration is enabled on first detection without a write.

`agentx config enable <id> --place-all` also places every skill the library holds into that configuration, as an install places it without `--copy`, so a configuration `copy_mode` records for a skill gets a copy of it and every other placement is a symlink; without the flag, enabling only decides where later installs go and places nothing. `--place-all` belongs to `enable` alone: `config disable --place-all` is exit code 1. The settings write and every placement it implies are one journaled mutation, since they are one thing the user asked for and an interrupted run may not leave a configuration enabled with half the library in it: the next command finishes that journal rather than undoing it. A placement the run skips is another matter and does leave part of the library placed, which the output and the `result` say. A placement path holding something agentx did not put there is left alone and skipped exactly as it is during an install: what is refused is a placement and not a skill, so the run still succeeds, the other skills land, the path is one `warning:` line naming it and the `result` counts it. A copy `copy_mode` records for the configuration that does not hold the library's version is kept and skipped the same way, with the two-line warning an install gives it, whose second line names the commands that replace it with the library version. The settings event comes first, then one `progress` of phase `place` per library skill, then the `rescan`, `total` being `<skills in the library> + 1`, then one `library_skill` event per library skill in name order with the placements the rescan found in this configuration. A configuration whose client reads the library has nothing to place: it already sees every library skill, and the run says so rather than making a second entry, in the output and in the `result` both, neither of which reports a placement it did not make.

```json
{
  "schema_version": 1,
  "label": "my-laptop",
  "accept_operations": false,
  "ignore_system_files": true,
  "disabled_configurations": ["windsurf"],
  "sources": [
    {
      "url": "https://github.com/example/skills",
      "alias": "https://github.com/example/skills-old",
      "pin": "v1.2.0",
      "access": "writable",
      "access_checked": "2026-09-18T10:00:00Z",
      "default_branch": "main",
      "last_fetched": "2026-09-18T10:00:00Z"
    },
    {
      "url": "ssh://git@github.com/me/agentx-forks",
      "account": true,
      "access_checked": "2026-09-18T10:00:00Z",
      "last_fetched": "2026-09-18T10:00:00Z"
    }
  ],
  "copy_mode": {
    "my-skill": ["cursor"]
  }
}
```

| Key | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `schema_version` | the settings schema version, currently `1` |
| `label` | the machine label; absent until set with `config set label` or `machine rename`, and reported as the hostname meanwhile |
| `accept_operations` | reserved; whether this machine executes operations queued for it |
| `ignore_system_files` | whether git ignores, in every skill directory, the files an operating system or an editor leaves there on its own; `true` by default, and a file without the key reads `true`. The list is `.DS_Store`, `._*`, `.AppleDouble`, `.LSOverride`, `Thumbs.db`, `ehthumbs.db`, `desktop.ini`, `.directory`, `.fuse_hidden*`, `.nfs*`, `*.swp`, `*.swo`, `*~` and `.#*`, each matched against a base name. While it is on, the account repo's `info/exclude` holds the list, one pattern per line; while it is off, the file is empty. `config set ignore_system_files` rewrites the file at once, and every git run over a skill directory brings it in line first, see [Git](#git) |
| `disabled_configurations` | configuration ids the user took out of the default set for explicit placements, sorted; every other detected configuration is enabled |
| `sources` | each source by canonical `url`, sorted by it, with optional `alias` (another URL of the same repository whose fetches map onto the canonical one, such as its old URL after a rename; reserved, nothing sets it, and nothing is ever pushed to it), optional `account` (`true` on the one entry that is the [account remote](#account-remote), which holds one branch per fork, `skills/<name>`, and which forks publish to; `agentx source add --account` writes it. Absent on every other source, a shared source, which holds skills in folders anywhere in the tree of one branch), optional `pin` (the ref the source is held at, absent when it follows the remote's default branch; the account remote, a whole repository, never has one), optional `access` (what git lets this machine do at the source: `writable`, or `read-only` when git answered with a denial it is known to give for want of rights; absent while it is unknown, which is everything else), optional `access_checked` (RFC 3339 in UTC, when the access was last checked, also when the check could not decide), optional `default_branch` (the branch the remote's `HEAD` named when it was last looked at; shown, and never used to choose a branch; the account remote is written with none) and `last_fetched` (RFC 3339 in UTC). At most one entry is the account remote, and no shared source names the repository the account remote names; written by `source add`, `source remove` and `source fetch`, which rewrites `last_fetched` and what its access check found, `access`, `access_checked` and `default_branch`, alone, as `skill check-updates` does for the sources it fetched and the update check of [serve](#serve) does for every source it fetched, a source no skill was installed from included, and by `skill add` when it adds or fetches the source it installs from, as `source add` does, see [Sources](#sources) |
| `copy_mode` | skill directory name to the configuration ids that receive a copy instead of a symlink, sorted; written by `agentx skill add --copy` and `agentx skill place --copy`, one write per run, and by `agentx skill remove`, which takes out the configurations it no longer places the skill in, whether it deleted a placement there or found none, and the whole entry when the skill leaves the machine. A skill with no copy anywhere leaves no entry behind |

Git config in the account repo holds only what git owns: remotes and tracking branches. Its `info/exclude` holds the `ignore_system_files` list and nothing else.

## Machine identity

The machine id names one computer across reinstalls. It is derived, in this order:

1. When `machine.json` exists in agentx home, its `id` is the machine id and the derivation is `random`. The file is `{"id": "<32 lowercase hex characters>"}`.
2. Otherwise, when a platform id exists, the machine id is HMAC-SHA256 with the key `agentx-machine-id/v1` over the message `<platform id> LF <uid>`, where `LF` is one newline byte and `<uid>` is the numeric user id in decimal, rendered as the first 32 lowercase hex characters of the MAC. The derivation is `platform`. The platform id is `AGENTX_PLATFORM_ID` when the variable is set, else the content of `/etc/machine-id` without surrounding whitespace on Linux, else `IOPlatformUUID` from `ioreg -rd1 -c IOPlatformExpertDevice` on macOS.
3. Otherwise 16 random bytes are generated once, under the lock, stored as hex in `machine.json`, and used from then on as in 1.

`agentx machine` prints the id, the label and the derivation. `agentx machine rename <label>` sets the label in the settings file. `agentx machine reset-id` writes a new random id to `machine.json`; from then on the id is random even where a platform id exists, until the file is deleted. Without `--json`, `machine` prints a `key  value` table with the rows `Machine id`, `Label` and `Derivation`, and the two mutations print one confirmation line: `✓ machine label is now <label>` or `✓ machine id is now <id>`.

## Lock and version file

Every command that changes agentx home takes an exclusive advisory `flock` on `lock` in agentx home without waiting, recovers any unfinished [mutation journal](#mutation-journal), does its writes through a journal of its own, rewrites `version` as its last step and releases the lock. `version` holds one decimal integer and a newline, incremented on every successful mutation (a missing file counts as 0); it is a change signal for watchers, not an ordering of snapshots. A command that finds the lock held exits with code 7 and a hint naming the lock file after at most 50 ms of retrying; it never waits for the holder. The one exception is a **take-back**: a command undoing, under a later hold, a write an earlier hold of its own already made. Giving up there would leave that write behind for good, since the lock is exactly what the command needs to clean up after itself, so a take-back waits for the holder for up to five seconds before it gives up – long enough for a queue of ordinary mutations, each of which holds the lock for one file write, and short enough that a lost lock never becomes a hang. A take-back leaves `version` untouched, like any step whose change nothing watching agentx home ever saw. The update check of the [serve](#serve) child waits too, for as long as serve runs, for both of its holds: it runs in the background on a timer, where nobody would run it again, and its waiting blocks nobody, since every other command still gives up on a lock it finds held. So does the last hold of a publish to a shared source, for up to five seconds as a take-back does: the source took the commit already, so giving up there would leave a published version unrecorded (see **Publishing a skill of a shared source** under [Skills](#skills)). While it holds the exclusive lock, a command keeps its process id in the lock file, so `agentx doctor` can name the holder. A failed mutation leaves `version` untouched. `agentx scan` holds a shared `flock` on the same file while it reads settings, the lineage and the filesystem, retrying for up to one second while a mutation holds the exclusive lock and exiting with code 7 after that; it never writes `version`. With `--handshake` it releases the shared lock before it starts or connects to any server, and writes `handshakes.json` afterwards under the exclusive lock, again without touching `version`. Other reading commands do not take the lock, except for the one write that stores a random machine id; that write does not touch `version`. A command that compares a skill directory with its base (`skill list`, `scan`, serve, `skill diff` and `adopt` among them) also writes, even without the exclusive lock and without touching `version`, the objects of that directory, which no ref holds, and the account repo's `info/exclude`, which follows `ignore_system_files`; see [Git](#git). Taking either lock creates agentx home and its `mutations` and `ops` directories when they are missing; nothing writes into `ops` yet.

## Mutation journal

Every replacement of a state file in agentx home is journaled, so that a process stopped at any point leaves either the old file or the new one and a later command can tell which and finish the job. This covers `settings.json` (`config set`, `config enable`, `config disable`, `machine rename`, `source add`, `source fetch`, `skill check-updates` and the update check of [serve](#serve), `source remove`, `import`), `machine.json` (`machine reset-id`, and the first command that stores a random machine id) and `handshakes.json` (`scan --handshake`). `version` is a signal, not state, and is rewritten atomically without a journal. Creating the account repo is not journaled: it builds the repository in a temporary directory and renames it into place.

An install changes more than state files: a lineage ref per skill, a library directory per skill and the placements that point at them. A removal and a later placement change the same kinds of thing the other way, an update replaces a library directory, or a fork's skill directory in its worktree, and the copies that held it, `skill place` replaces displaced placements, an update check moves candidate and upstream-removed refs beside its settings write, a fork moves a library directory into a fork's worktree, `skill place` and serve's reconciliation check a fork's worktree out again, `skill add` with no source checks out a skill another machine published, superseding a managed skill's library directory, and `skill place --force` adopts a directory in a fork's way by moving it, and an update moves import branches and fork branches, replaces library directories, a fork's skill directory and the copies that held them, removes the worktree of a pending merge it applies, and deletes the candidates it applied; the merge it leaves pending for a skill whose edits conflicted with its update is set up after its journal, outside it, under the same hold of the lock (see **Conflicts** under [Skills](#skills)). Those are journaled as `steps` in the same journal, one journal however many skills the run touches, each decided from the live state and safe to repeat, so that one interrupted run is finished rather than half undone. The `ref` steps run in two phases, and recovery runs them in the same two: every ref the journal creates or moves goes first, before any path step, and every ref it deletes goes last, after the path steps and the state files. A ref a journal writes can be written again from the journal, and one it deletes cannot be brought back once the journal is gone, so the deletions run only after everything that could still refuse has not. Between the two phases go the path steps in the order they are recorded, then the state files. A removal that is refused therefore still has the skill's lineage, and abandoning its journal leaves a managed skill managed rather than turning it into an unmanaged one; an update whose library directory changed under it, so that its `remove` step refuses, has moved its import branch but still has its candidate ref, and the directory keeps what it held. Abandoning that journal leaves a candidate ref that names the commit the import branch already holds: that is no update, so no command shows or applies it (no `candidate` in `library_skill`, no `update available`, nothing for `skill diff --update`, `skill update` or `--all`, no `update_available`), and the next update check deletes the ref. The refs of one phase in one repository are read in one call and moved in one transaction, so that a journal never holds some of its branches written and others not, and an install of thirty skills is two git processes for its refs rather than sixty; a journal that both writes and deletes refs, as an update and an update check do, takes two such transactions. A step is `ref` (point a ref at a commit, with an expected old value, empty for a ref that must not exist yet; a `new` that is empty deletes the ref instead, still with the value it is expected to hold, so that a branch something else moved is refused rather than dropped; an `old` and a `new` that are the same commit move nothing and hold the journal to that commit, so that recovery finishes a change made for a version only while the branch still names it), `publish` (rename a staged directory into place), `link` (create a symlink placement) `remove` (take a path out of the way, retaining what it held), `move` (rename a live directory, the user's content, to a path where nothing is yet, as a fork in its place moves the library directory into its worktree, and as `skill place --force` moves an orphaned worktree's skill directory out of the way of the worktree and back; nothing is staged or retained; the command refuses before its journal exists when the directory no longer holds the fingerprint it captured under the lock, and recovery moves the directory while it is still a directory, edited since or not, since a move only takes the user's own content to the place the journal names, and refuses a source that is gone or is no longer a directory) or `worktree` (make a path a linked worktree of a fork's branch, added with `worktree add --no-checkout --lock --reason "agentx fork"`, and align the worktree's index with the branch tip, `git reset -q` in it, touching no file; a path that already is that worktree has its index aligned again, since nothing on disk says whether that ran). Content a step stages or retains lives in a hidden directory beside the live path, `.agentx-staged-<id>-<n>` and `.agentx-retained-<id>-<n>`, not in `mutations/`: the rename that publishes or displaces it then stays on one filesystem, and discovery skips hidden entries, so no agent client sees a half-written skill. Retained content is dropped only once every live path holds its new state and it still hashes to what the journal captured; content that changed meanwhile is kept and named, as a `warning:` line on stderr giving both the live path and the hidden directory the content was kept in, since nothing else will ever mention it.
