A fork whose content to place, its skill directory, the directory adopted or the tip's skill directory, holds no `SKILL.md` is not a skill and is exit code 5 before anything changes: `<path> holds no SKILL.md, so <name> is no skill to place and nothing was changed`, with the hint `add a SKILL.md to <path>, then run 'agentx skill place <name>' again`, or, for a tip, `<name>'s branch skills/<name> holds no SKILL.md in <dir>, so there is no skill to place and nothing was changed`. `--force` on a fork with nothing in the way is exit code 6, `<name> is a fork with nothing in the way of its worktree or its library symlink, so --force has nothing to adopt`, with the hint `place it without --force with 'agentx skill place <name>'`. Without `--json`, after the placement rows, one line says what was put back or adopted: `✓ checked <name>'s worktree out again from its branch`, `✓ laid <name>'s skill directory out again from its branch`, `✓ put <name>'s library symlink back`, `✓ repaired the pointers of <name>'s worktree`, `✓ adopted <path> as <name>'s worktree; its content is the skill's unpublished edits` or `✓ moved <path> into <name>'s worktree; its content is the skill's unpublished edits`; the result summary ends with the same words after `; `.

**Where symlinks make paths overlap, `skill place` refuses.** It removes and writes whole directories, and a symlink can make any of them part of something else, so rather than follow links to find out what, it is exit code 6, with or without `--force` and before anything is changed, wherever symlinks make what it changes overlap what it must leave alone. The paths it changes are every place it writes, a displaced directory, a missing place and the library's symlink where a copy belongs; a place two configurations share is one path, and a place this machine cannot read is skipped, not changed. Each is compared where it really is, every symlink on the way to it resolved, so a client's skills directory that is a symlink to a directory elsewhere is simply followed there. The rule is read when the run is planned, again under the lock, and once more right before anything is staged:

1. A run that removes a displaced directory finds the skill's library entry, `<library>/<name>`, a real directory, as agentx installs every managed skill. An entry that is a symlink is `<path> is a symlink to <target>, not the directory agentx installed, so nothing was placed`, with the hint `replace the link with the directory it leads to, then run 'agentx skill place <name>' again`. A run that only writes placements makes them from the entry as it is.
2. No path the run changes is, lies inside or holds the library, or the directory a library entry leads to, an entry that is a symlink taken where it resolves. Nor do two paths it changes, or one of them and another configuration's own place of the skill, whatever that holds, lie one inside the other: removing the outer one would take the inner one with it, and writing the inner one would change what the outer one was judged to hold. The message names both paths: `<path> lies inside the library <library>, and placing it would change what the library holds, so nothing was placed`, or `<path> lies inside <directory>, where the library entry <entry> leads, and placing it would change what the library holds, so nothing was placed`, with `holds` in place of `lies inside` where the path holds the other, and beginning `<path> is the library <library>` or `the library entry <entry> leads to <path>` where the two are one; for two paths one inside the other it is `<path> lies inside <path>, and placing one would change the other, so nothing was placed`. The hint is `replace the symlink that joins them with the files it leads to, or move one of them elsewhere, then run 'agentx skill place <name>' again`.
3. A run that removes a displaced directory finds no symlink at any depth in the skill's library directory: an import never holds one, so one there is an edit made by hand, which `skill place` does not reason about. The message is `the library directory <path> holds the symlink <link>, which no version agentx installs holds, so nothing was placed`, with the hint `replace the link with the files it leads to, or see what changed with 'agentx skill diff <name>' and get the original back by removing the skill and adding it again, then run 'agentx skill place <name>' again`; a run that only writes placements is not affected.

Every command the hints of this section name carries the `--to` and `--copy` that were given. Nothing else is followed: `skill place` does not search the disk, another skill's directory included, for a symlink that leads into or through a path it changes. One that leads into a directory `--force` discards is the user's and leads nowhere afterwards; one that leads into a displaced directory holding the library's content still resolves to the same content through the symlink that replaces it; and one spelled through such a directory and out again with `..`, a library entry among them, takes its `..` from where that symlink leads.

It is one journaled mutation under the exclusive lock, and the import branch of a managed skill does not move. What `skill place` replaces is what the paths held when it judged them: the library entry, what the library directory holds, the settings and every place are read first, and read again under the lock with the lineage branches. A branch that moved is exit code 6, `the import branch refs/heads/managed/<name> moved while <name> was being placed, so nothing was changed`, and any other difference is exit code 6, `<name> or its placements changed while it was being placed, so nothing was changed`, with the hint `run 'agentx skill place <name>' again`: a directory edited meanwhile is never discarded with the rest. The journal holds each place's steps in the order the configurations are detected, then the settings when `copy_mode` changes, and, for a managed skill, a `ref` step whose old and new value are the import commit, which moves nothing and holds recovery to that version; it runs before the path steps whatever its position, as every ref step that deletes nothing does. Every `remove` expects the fingerprint the run judged, and the content it retains is dropped only when it still hashes to it, see the [mutation journal](#mutation-journal). A place whose content this machine cannot read, or that it cannot write, is left as it is and counted as skipped, with the warning of a placement that cannot be made; the rest of the run still lands. A place this machine cannot look at at all, under a directory it may not search, has no drift, as the listing reads it, and is not replaced. A run with nothing to write makes no mutation. A managed skill whose library directory is gone is exit code 6, with the message of the listing's warning for it followed by `, so there is no skill to place`, and the two ways out of that warning as its hint.

**Checking for updates.** `agentx skill check-updates` looks for newer upstream versions of the managed skills and forks and records what it finds; it never applies one, and it writes nothing to the library or to a placement. It covers every managed skill whose lineage agentx can read and every fork with a base version, whose source the settings hold and whose directory the library holds. A skill whose source was removed is left as it is, its candidate and its marker included, and its source is not fetched: nothing names the source to fetch, and adding it back is the user's to decide. A fork is compared by its base version, the import commit its history names (see **Forks**), exactly as a managed skill at that version would be, whatever its own commits changed since: an update is a newer upstream version, and merging it is what keeps those commits. Its base is read by the one `git log` walk of the forks' histories a listing runs, none when every fork's tip is an import commit, and serve keeps what it read by tip. A skill `skill new` made, a fork of an unmanaged or a plugin's skill, and a fork whose history does not say which import is its base have no upstream and are not checked. A managed skill whose library directory is gone is not checked either, `skill list` naming it in a warning. With an account remote set, your own skills placed on this machine, every fork, are checked against what the account remote holds of them too, see below. A machine where no skill is covered fetches nothing, spawns no git when the settings hold no source at all, prints `Nothing to check: no managed skill or fork comes from a source added on this machine.` and exits 0.

The sources those skills came from are fetched once each, by the fetch of `source fetch` (see **Fetch again** under [Sources](#sources)): at the pin the settings hold, in the user's git environment, in parallel at most four at a time and outside the lock, after one early hold of it that recovers an unfinished [mutation journal](#mutation-journal) and brings the remotes in line with the settings; each source ends with a `progress` event of phase `fetch` carrying its URL, `total` being the number of sources. Each skill is then compared with what its source holds at the fetched commit by tree id, never by reading a file or a content hash. The fetch lists every directory of the commit that holds a `SKILL.md`, the hidden ones and `node_modules` included, with its tree, and the `for-each-ref` that reads the lineage reads the tree of every import commit: a skill whose directory's tree, wrapped under the upstream's directory name as the import commit wraps it, is the import commit's tree is current, at no git process of its own. A skill whose subpath the commit holds no directory for, or a directory without a `SKILL.md`, is upstream removed. The rest are read as an install reads them (see **What is read**): the whole commit listed once and one history walk for the upstream commits of all of them, then the import tree each version would get computed in process. A version whose only difference from the base version is what an import leaves out, a symlink or a submodule, has the base version's import tree and is current too, so that a skill holding a symlink never shows an update it does not have. For what is left, the blobs this machine does not hold are fetched in one batch in the user's environment, the files are read in one `cat-file` and the content hashes computed, and the import commit of every newer version is written through one `fast-import` onto staging refs `refs/agentx/importing/<run>/<n>`, outside the lock: the commit an install of that version writes, by the same code. A version an install would refuse for what it holds, such as an entry agentx will not lay out or a file the source did not serve, is refused for its skill alone, with the reason and the exit code the install gives; a name the frontmatter gives it now is not among them, the skill keeping its own through an update. The git processes of a check therefore grow with its sources and not with its skills: none for a skill it compares, and for all the skills that changed together one round of reads, one fetch of blobs, one import and, at the end, one read of what the updates change.

What it found is recorded in one mutation under the lock, the only hold of it after the network. The lineage is read again under it, and a skill whose import branch no longer names the commit it was compared with, a fork whose history no longer names the base it was compared with, or a skill that is gone, gets nothing: it was compared with something it no longer is. A fork's own commits made meanwhile change nothing of that. Every other skill's candidate ref `refs/agentx/candidate/<name>` is pointed at the import commit of the newer version, deleted for a skill that is current or upstream removed, and left where it is for a skill whose newer version was refused or could not be read; its marker `refs/agentx/upstream-removed/<name>` is pointed at the fetched commit for a skill that is upstream removed, or deleted otherwise, a refused skill included, since its source holds it again, each with the value it holds now as its expected old value, so that a ref something else moved is refused rather than overwritten. `last_fetched` is written, now, for every source that fetched; a source removed while the check fetched is not written back, has the ref its fetch published taken away again, as for `source fetch`, and its skills get nothing. A fetch or a read of such a source that failed is not reported either: the removal takes the source's remote and staging refs away, which is often what made it fail, and the check leaves the skills of a removed source alone. A check whose every source was removed while it fetched says there is nothing to check, as above, and exits 0. The write bumps `version` once, and the staging refs are deleted after it. A fork never gets a marker: its upstream is only where newer versions come from, so a fork whose upstream no longer holds it just has no update, its candidate deleted as for a skill that is current, and it is not counted as upstream removed. Only a check writes a candidate or a marker; a removal of the skill deletes both, and an update that applies a candidate deletes it (see **Updating** below); a source that could not be fetched this time, and one removed, leave both where they are. A later check that finds a newer version still moves the candidate to it, and one that finds the base version again, a version whose import is the base version's, or no skill at all deletes it.

The check emits one `update_available` event per skill it could check that holds a candidate afterwards, sorted by name, whether this check pinned the candidate or an earlier one did, with the files the candidate changes against the base version: those of every candidate are read at once, in one `git diff-tree --stdin -r -z --no-renames --name-status` over their pairs of trees, and the names the candidates' `SKILL.md` files give in one `cat-file`. A check that moves a candidate warns once for each entry the newer version leaves out of its import, naming it by its path in the source as an install does, `<path> is not a regular file and is left out of the update of <name>`, and once when that version names the skill otherwise, `<name>: the update names the skill "<upstream name>"; updating it keeps the name <name>`; a check that moves nothing repeats neither. A source that could not be fetched is one `warning:` naming the source, the reason and the skills it left unchecked, `<message>; not checked: <name>, ...`, the message and the hint being those `source fetch` gives the same failure, and a version that cannot be imported is one naming its skill, `<name>: <message>`. A skill a warning names gets no `update_available` on that check, while a candidate an earlier check pinned for it stays, and `skill list` still shows it. The check goes on past both and records everything else, then ends with the exit code every failure agrees on, or 3 when they disagree, and an `error` naming them: `could not check ` and the warning for one, `could not check <url> (<name>, ...), <name>, ...` for several, whose hint is `the warnings name each failure`, followed by the hint a `source fetch` of several sources gives when every failure is a source's. An update found is no failure: a check that could check everything exits 0.

**What your other machines published.** With an account remote set (see [Account remote](#account-remote)) and at least one of your own skills placed on this machine, a fork with a worktree or a library entry here, the check also fetches the account remote, once and whole, as `skill update` does (see **Fetching** under [Account remote](#account-remote)), outside the lock and once the shared sources are fetched, as one more source of the same run: its remote is brought in line with the settings in the same hold of the lock as theirs, and its `progress` event of phase `fetch`, carrying its URL, counts on from theirs to the same `total`, whether its fetch succeeded or not; [serve](#serve)'s check does the same on every tick, with no `progress`, and, when an import of settings left the account remote unconfigured in the account repo, configures it waiting for the lock, as it takes the lock everywhere. A skill is compared by commit: one whose branch on the account remote holds the tip of its branch here, or that the remote holds no branch of, has nothing new; otherwise one `git merge-base` per skill finds the last commit both share, and a remote branch that is that commit, which this machine already holds, as one it published and moved on from, has nothing new either, and neither has one that shares no commit with it, which `skill update` refuses to merge. A skill whose directory cannot be told, a branch whose tip holds other than one directory, or whose `git merge-base` fails, is a failure naming that skill alone, as a skill whose newer version cannot be imported is, and costs the check nothing else. Every other skill has an update: what its remote branch changed since that commit, read for every such skill at once, one `git rev-parse` of their trees and one `git diff-tree --stdin -r -z --no-renames --name-status` over their pairs, each path taken relative to the skill's directory. It is one `update_available` among the others, in name order, naming the account remote as its `source` and the remote branch's tip as its `candidate` (see the event above), and `skill update` takes it in (see **Updating a fork**); a skill that also has a newer upstream version gets both events. Nothing is recorded for it beyond what the fetch brought, the remote-tracking branches, and it is announced only for a skill whose branch, read again under the lock the check writes with, is still the commit it was compared with: one that an update took the remote's commits into, or that was removed, while the check ran gets none. The account remote counts as one source in the summary, the skills compared with it as skills checked, each once, a skill that both a shared source and the account remote checked included, and its `last_fetched` is written with the shared sources', in the same mutation, now: a check that fetched only the account remote still takes the lock once to write it. An account remote git cannot reach is a failure as an unreachable shared source is: one `warning:` `the account remote <url>: cannot reach it: <reason>; not checked: <name>, ...`, the refusal reading `could not check the account remote <url>: ...` when it is the only failure, the shared sources still checked and recorded, and exit code 3 unless other failures disagree, with the message, hint and `error` above.

Without `--json` a check that fetched a source prints `✓ checked <n> skills from <n> sources: <n> updates available` (`1 skill`, `1 source`, `1 update available`, `no update available`), counting the skills it recorded a verdict for, followed by `, <n> upstream removed` when it found any; then, for each update, `  <name>  update available  <short upstream commit> -> <short candidate upstream commit>  <n> files` and one row per file under it, `    <status>  <path>`, the path quoted as [Streams](#streams) prescribes; then, for each skill upstream removed, `  <name>  upstream removed  <url>[/<subpath>] at <short fetched commit>`, the name and the upstream sanitised; then `Apply an update with agentx skill update <name>, or read it first with agentx skill diff <name> --update.` when there is an update. An update another machine published is the line `  <name>  published from another machine  <short commit here> -> <short commit on the account remote>  <n> files`, with its files under it, and when every update listed is one, the last line is `Apply an update with agentx skill update <name>.` alone, since `skill diff --update` shows only an upstream's. The `result` carries the first line's words. The update check of [serve](#serve) is this check, run on a timer over every source of the settings rather than only those a managed skill came from.

`agentx skill diff <name> --update` shows what the candidate changes: the upstream directory of the import commit the branch points at against that of the candidate, two trees of the account repo, compared by two reads run at once, `git diff-tree -r -z --no-renames --name-status` for the status of every file and `git diff-tree -r -p --no-renames --no-color` for the diffs, and emitted as `diff` events as **Comparing with the base** emits them. It reads nothing else, writes nothing and never reaches the network, so it shows what the check pinned rather than what the source holds now, and the library plays no part: an edit made there shows in `skill diff` alone. Every path is relative to the skill's directory. Its line is `the update of <name> at <short candidate upstream commit> differs from its base version at <short upstream commit> in <n> files`. A fork's update is compared with the fork's base version in the same way, the import commit its history names against the candidate: what merging it brings in, not how it differs from the fork's own commits. A skill with no candidate, or one whose candidate its import branch, or a fork's base, already holds, is exit code 6, `no update of <name> is known`, with a hint to run `agentx skill check-updates`; a fork with no upstream is exit code 6, `<name> has no upstream to update from`, and one whose history does not say which import is its base is exit code 8; the other refusals are those of `skill diff`.

**Updating.** `agentx skill update <name>` applies the update the last update check pinned for a managed skill or a fork, its candidate `refs/agentx/candidate/<name>`, and `agentx skill update --all` applies every one there is; a name and `--all` together, or neither, is exit code 1. `agentx skill update <name> --abort` gives up the skill's pending merge instead, see **A merge pending**, and `--abort` with `--all` is exit code 1. It is the one command that applies an update, and nothing applies one on its own. For a managed skill it never reaches the network: the version it lays out is the candidate the check wrote into the account repo, so what it applies is exactly what `skill diff <name> --update` showed. A fork's update reaches the account remote first, when one is set, and `--all` then also takes in what other machines published to every fork placed here, see **Updating a fork**. Before it reads a branch or a directory it finishes any unfinished journal, taking the lock only when there is one (see [Mutation journal](#mutation-journal)): an update stopped midway has moved its import branch to the candidate first, so the skill would otherwise read as updated while its library directory still held the version replaced, or held none. Running the update again after one stopped therefore finishes it and then answers for the machine as the recovery left it, the skill up to date. A skill whose library directory holds its base version, one `state` reads as `current`, updates by replacement; one edited since it was installed, by any tool, merges its edits with the update, see **Updating an edited skill** below. `current` is decided as for `state`, by the tree id in process and then by git over a throwaway index loaded from the base version, so a skill whose only extra files are ones git ignores updates too. What the directory holds is captured first, as the fingerprint a journal compares, and so are the files git ignores in it, as `git ls-files --others --ignored --exclude-standard -z` lists them over that throwaway index, which still hold under the lock while the fingerprint does. The candidate's version is read out of the account repo outside the lock, one `ls-tree -r -t -z` of the candidate per skill, whose upstream directory name is taken off every path, and one `cat-file --batch` of the blobs of every skill of the run. Under the lock the import branch, a fork branch of the name, the candidate and the upstream-removed marker of every skill are read again in one `for-each-ref`, and so are the settings, each library directory and whether a merge is pending, and a skill is answered for in the order it is judged before the lock: a branch that moved, or a fork of the name, is exit code 6, `the import branch refs/heads/managed/<name> moved while <name> was being updated, so nothing was changed`; a merge pending is the refusal of a merge pending below; a source removed meanwhile is the refusal of a removed source below; a marker that appeared is the `upstream removed` refusal below; a candidate that moved or went, as a check run meanwhile moves or deletes it, is exit code 6, `the update candidate refs/agentx/candidate/<name> moved while <name> was being updated, so nothing was changed`; and a library directory that changed since it was captured is exit code 6, `<name> changed while it was being updated, so nothing was changed`, with the hint `run 'agentx skill update <name>' again to update it as it is now`, so an edit made meanwhile is never replaced. None of them writes a journal. The candidate's version is laid out in a hidden directory beside the library directory and read back as git would record it, and only a directory whose tree is the candidate's is published: no ignore rule, attribute or filter changes a byte, and every file keeps the mode the version records. A version is laid out with every file `0644`, or `0755` when the version records it executable, and every directory `0755`, less the umask. The files git ignores in the library directory are then copied into it unchanged, each with its bytes and mode and a symlink as a link, but for a path the new version holds, whose file is the new version's, as `git checkout` leaves it, and for a path under a file or a symlink the new version holds where the ignored file's directory was, which is dropped, as `git checkout` drops it, and never written through the link; what the library directory held, those files included, stays in the directory the journal retains until the update is complete. The journal, one for the whole run, holds for each skill a `ref` step moving `refs/heads/managed/<name>` from the import commit it held to the candidate, with the former as its expected old value; a `remove` of the library directory, which retains what it held, and a `publish` of the staged version; those two steps for each copy the update refreshes; and a `ref` step deleting the candidate ref, with the candidate as its expected old value. The move runs before every path step and the deletion after them (see [Mutation journal](#mutation-journal)), so a refusal on the way leaves the candidate where it was and the library directory with what it held. The copies `copy_mode` records for the skill are judged by what each holds now, as `state` judges a library directory, the tree id first and then git with the copy as its work tree, since nothing records what a copy held when it was placed: a copy that already holds the new version is left alone and says nothing, one that holds the version replaced, the library directory as it was or, for an edited skill, the base version a copy placed before the edits holds, is refreshed with the new one through the same retaining `remove` and `publish`, its fingerprint checked again when the step runs, and keeps the files git ignores in it, never the library's, and one holding anything else was edited where it is and is kept byte for byte, counted as skipped and named with the warning for a kept copy under **Placements**; a copy that cannot be read or staged is left as it is and counted as skipped, with `warning: cannot refresh <path>: <cause>; the copy was left as it is`. A path two configurations share, as Zencoder and Zenflow share one skills directory, is judged once: a copy refreshed there counts for each configuration `copy_mode` records it for, and a copy kept or skipped there is one warning and one skipped placement. A symlink placement names the library directory and needs nothing. The import commit the branch held stays reachable through the branch's reflog. Afterwards a skill updated by replacement is `current` at its new base version, unless a file it kept is one the new version's own `.gitignore` no longer ignores, and every updated skill carries no `candidate`, `skill list` shows no `update available` for it, and a check that finds the source still at that version pins nothing. The skill keeps its library name, its import branch and its placements whatever the newer version's `SKILL.md` calls it, and the `SKILL.md` is laid out as the upstream wrote it: a version that names the skill otherwise gives, once it is applied, the warning the check gave, `<name>: the update names the skill "<upstream name>"; updating it keeps the name <name>`.

A skill is judged in this order, and the first answer that applies is the command's. A name the library does not hold as a skill, and no branch names, is exit code 5, with the message of `skill diff`. A skill with no branch is exit code 6, `<name> is not managed by agentx, so it has no upstream to update from`, and a fork is judged as under **Updating a fork** below. A managed skill whose library directory is gone is exit code 6, the warning under **Listing** followed by `, so there is nothing to update`, its two ways out as the hint. A branch that records no version agentx can read is exit code 6. A skill whose source has no settings entry is exit code 5, `<name> was installed from <url>, which was removed from this machine, so it is not updated`, with the hint `run 'agentx source add <url>' to add it again`, the URL as under **Id.** in [Sources](#sources): its candidate stays, and applies once the source is back. `--all` skips such a skill instead, see below. A skill the last check found `upstream removed` is exit code 6, `the last update check found that the source of <name> no longer holds it, so it is kept as it is and never updated`, whose hint names `skill check-updates` and `skill remove`. A skill with no candidate, one whose trailers agentx cannot read, or one whose candidate is the commit its import branch already holds has nothing to update: the command exits 0 with `<name> is up to date as of the last update check; run 'agentx skill check-updates' to look again`, which the text prints with the command unquoted, and emits no `library_skill`, since nothing changed. A skill with a candidate whose library entry is a symlink is exit code 6, `<path> is a symlink to <target>; an update replaces the library directory and would drop the link without touching the files it leads to`, since the update replaces the entry and would drop the link, whatever the directory it leads to holds. A skill with a candidate whose library directory holds something git cannot record, a nested repository say, is exit code 6, `<name> holds <path>, which git cannot record`, naming every such path, with the hint `an update would discard it with no record of it anywhere; move it out of the skill, then run 'agentx skill update <name>' again`. A skill with a merge pending is answered from its worktree once it is past the `upstream removed` refusal, whatever its candidate, see **A merge pending** below.

`--all` covers every managed skill that holds a candidate agentx can read and its import branch does not already hold, every fork that holds one other than its base version and, when the run fetched the account remote, every fork placed here that another machine published to or whose merge is pending, see **Updating a fork**, in name order, and is one run: one hold of the lock, one journal and one rescan for the managed skills, then one hold of the lock and one journal for each fork, and the run's refusals and conflicts are counted over both. A modified skill is merged as it is on its own, in the same run and the same journal. A skill whose source has no settings entry, found so before the lock or under it, is skipped, with a `warning:` giving the message of that refusal above and a second line giving its hint, `run 'agentx source add <url>' to add it again`; skipping it is no failure, since removing a source is the user's own choice and a check leaves the candidate of such a skill in place for as long as the source is gone, so a failure would recur on every run. With `--json` that warning is one `log` event of level `warn` whose message holds both lines, joined by `; `. A skill that holds something git cannot record is not skipped but refused, as below. Any other skill that refuses, before the lock or under it, costs only itself, and so does one whose merge conflicts, which is left pending with its `conflict` event and its conflicts in the text, and one whose merge pending still has files to resolve, reported the same way, while one resolved is applied with the rest: a `warning:` names it with its message, the rest of the run lands, and the run ends with the exit code every refusal agrees on, 4 when every skill it gave up on was left with a merge pending, 6 when they disagree, and an `error` naming each, `<n> of <m> skills could not be updated: <name>: <message>; ...`, as a batch install does (see **A skill that cannot be installed**); one refusal in a run that updated nothing is answered as that skill on its own would be. The `result` of such a run carries the `error`'s message and no copy counts: what it did for each skill it updated is in that skill's `library_skill` event and in the warnings for the copies it kept or skipped. A new version that cannot be laid out and read back beside its library directory is no refusal of one skill but a failure of the library: it ends the whole run before a journal is written, and nothing is changed. A skill with no candidate is not covered, but for those forks, an `upstream removed` one included, since a check that marks a skill deletes its candidate. A run left with nothing to update once the account step has run, with nothing pulled or failed, prints `Nothing to update: no managed skill or fork has an update as of the last update check. Run agentx skill check-updates to look again.` and exits 0, with the `result` summary `nothing to update: no managed skill or fork has an update as of the last update check; run 'agentx skill check-updates' to look again`.

After the mutation the machine is read again and the command emits one `library_skill` event per skill it updated, sorted by name, carrying every placement as `skill list` reports them, and a `result` of `updated <name> from <short upstream commit> to <short candidate upstream commit>`, then ` and merged its edits cleanly` for an edited skill, or ` with the merge you resolved` for one whose pending merge it applied, which for a fork's merge that the account step left pending and that keeps the fork's base replaces the versions too, as in `updated <name> with the merge you resolved`, then, for a fork, `, committed as <short merge commit>`, then `, <n> copy placements refreshed` and `, <n> placements skipped` when there were any; with `--all`, `updated <n> skills`, then `, <n> edited skills merged cleanly` (`, 1 edited skill merged cleanly` for one) when it merged any, then `, <n> copy placements refreshed` and `, <n> placements skipped`, added up over every skill it updated, when there were any, then `, <n> skills from removed sources skipped` (`, 1 skill from a removed source skipped` for one) when there were any, or `no skill was updated` followed by the same counts when every skill was skipped, `no skill was updated from upstream` when the run's account step pulled a fork, see **Updating a fork**. Without `--json` the line is printed after `✓ `, the name sanitised, but for the line of a run that updated nothing, which is printed as it is; `--all` follows its line with one row per skill it updated, `  <name>  <short upstream commit> -> <short candidate upstream commit>`, then `edits merged cleanly` for an edited skill, or `the merge you resolved`, then `committed as <short merge commit>` for a fork, and what the update did to its copies when it did anything; a merge you resolved is not counted among the edited skills merged cleanly. The conflicts of every merge the run left pending follow, see below.

**Updating an edited skill.** A skill whose library directory was edited since it was installed, by any tool, one `state` reads as `modified`, keeps its edits: the update merges them with the candidate, three-way, and git does the merge. Outside the lock, after it was judged as above, the tree git wrote of the directory as `state` reads it, over the throwaway index loaded from the base version, is wrapped in a one-entry tree under the upstream's directory name as the [import commit](#skills) wraps a version, by one `mktree`, and committed by one `commit-tree` with the import commit the branch points at as its one parent: that commit is "mine". Then one `git -c merge.directoryRenames=false -c merge.conflictStyle=zdiff3 merge-tree --write-tree -z --no-messages --merge-base=<import commit> <mine> <candidate>` in the isolated environment merges the two, the base version given as the merge base so that git looks for no other: files added, deleted or renamed on either side follow git's rules, and a conflict is written in zdiff3 style, the base between mine and theirs. It is the one `merge-tree` of the update: a clean merge is laid out from its tree, and a merge that conflicts is set up from its tree and its list of conflicted files. git's detection of a renamed directory is off, on every merge, so a file added on one side inside a directory the other side renamed stays where it was added, beside the renamed directory, rather than being moved, or conflicting, on git's guess. A file git ignores is not in mine, and a clean merge carries it over as a replacement does. A directory that changes while git reads it is found under the lock, by the fingerprint captured before, as above. A candidate that holds the skill under another upstream directory name than the branch is exit code 8 with a hint to run `skill check-updates`. Neither mine nor the tree `merge-tree` writes is held by a ref, see [Git](#git).

A clean merge is applied as a replacement is, the merged version, the skill's directory in the tree `merge-tree` wrote, laid out in place of the candidate's, in the same journal and with the same steps: the import branch moves to the candidate, the library directory is retained and replaced, the files git ignores in it carried over, the copies are judged as above, and the candidate ref is deleted last. The skill stays `modified` afterwards, its directory holding the new version with the edits on it, unless the edits were the update's own changes, and `skill diff` shows the edits against the new base version. The text and the `result` say ` and merged its edits cleanly` after the two commits.

**Conflicts.** A merge that conflicts changes nothing any client reads: the library directory, every placement, the import branch and the candidate are left exactly as they were, and no file with conflict markers in it is ever written where an agent reads. The merge is left pending instead, as an ordinary Git merge in progress in a Git worktree of the account repo, `<agentx home>/merges/<name>`. Under the lock, once the inputs are read again as above and the run's journal, when it has one, is applied, the worktree is set up in this order: `git worktree add --detach --lock --reason "agentx pending merge" <agentx home>/merges/<name> <mine>` adds it, detached at mine; `git read-tree --reset -u <tree>` in the worktree lays the tree the `merge-tree` above wrote, conflict markers and all, into the index and the work tree, and `git update-index -z --index-info` puts the stages it listed in the index in place of each conflicted file, so the worktree holds exactly the conflicts the update was judged by, whatever git version runs; then `MERGE_MSG`, `update <name> from <short upstream commit> to <short candidate upstream commit>, keeping its edits`, `ORIG_HEAD`, mine, as `git merge` writes it, and `MERGE_HEAD`, the candidate, are written where `git rev-parse --git-path` says git keeps them for the worktree. The skill's own `.gitattributes` applies in the worktree as in any Git checkout, so a file it converts is written there converted; its merge attributes, `merge=union` say, are not read by `merge-tree` in the account repo, so they change nothing of what conflicts. When any of it fails or the run is stopped, the worktree is removed again with `git worktree remove -f -f`; a failure is git's error, exit code 8, and a stopped run reports `interrupted`, exit code 9. Setting a merge up writes no journal and bumps `version`. The command emits a `conflict` event and exits 4, `<name> conflicts with its update in <n> files, so the merge is pending and the library directory was left as it is`, with the hint `resolve it with git in <path> ('git add' each file you resolved, or 'git checkout --ours|--theirs <file>' then 'git add'; 'git commit' is optional), then run 'agentx skill update <name>' again to apply it, or 'agentx skill update <name> --abort' to give it up`, `<path>` being the skill's directory in the worktree, `<agentx home>/merges/<name>/<upstream directory>`, which the files listed are relative to.

Which files conflict is read from `merge-tree`'s own list of conflicted files, `<mode> <blob> <stage>` and the path, stage 1 the base, 2 mine and 3 theirs, each path below the upstream's directory name taken relative to the skill's directory. agentx reads no file of the worktree for it, and never parses or rewrites the conflict markers git wrote: a front end shows a file that conflicts by feeding two of its versions to a diff tool, and the user resolves it with git. It sizes them, on every merge, of a managed skill or a fork: when a merge conflicts, the blobs of every version of every regular file that conflicts are read in one `cat-file --batch`, and when a line of one starts with a run of seven or more of one marker character, `<`, `=`, `>` or `|`, as a Markdown rule or a quoted conflict does, the merge is run once more with markers one longer than the longest such run, through `-c core.attributesFile=<a temporary file holding * conflict-marker-size=<n>>` given after the isolated environment's own, the `conflict-marker-size` attribute git documents. So every marker git writes tells itself apart from every line of the skill, which stays as it is, and `git status`, `git diff` and the resolution in the worktree see the markers as the merge wrote them. A merge with no such line runs one `merge-tree`. A binary file or a symlink that conflicts has no markers to size: `merge-tree` leaves mine's version of it whole in the tree the worktree is laid from, and its three versions are the stages in the index and the blobs the `conflict` event names, so the user takes one side with `git checkout --ours|--theirs <file>`.

Without `--json` the conflicts of each skill are printed after what the run updated: `<name> conflicts with its update from <short upstream commit> to <short candidate upstream commit> in <n> files`, then one line per file in path order, `<path>: <status>`, `<status>` being what `git status` says of an unmerged path, us being the library directory and them the update: `both modified` for a file with all three versions, `both added` for one with mine and theirs, `deleted by them` for one with the base and mine, `deleted by us` for one with the base and theirs, `added by us` and `added by them` for one with mine or theirs alone, and `both deleted` for one with the base alone, as a file moved to two places leaves the path it was moved from. The error follows on stderr.

**A merge pending.** A skill whose worktree `<agentx home>/merges/<name>` exists has a merge pending. It is an ordinary Git merge in progress, so `git status`, `git diff`, `git checkout --ours|--theirs`, `git add` and `git commit` work in the worktree as in any merge, and it survives every restart; `skill list` shows it, `merge pending`, and its `library_skill` carries `pending_merge: true`, both read from the directory with no git process.

`agentx skill update <name>` run again, or `--all` for a skill that still has a candidate, reads the worktree before the lock: `git ls-files -u -z`, `git log -2 --first-parent --format='%H %T %P' HEAD`, the `MERGE_HEAD` file where `git rev-parse --git-path` says git keeps it and, when nothing is unmerged, `git write-tree`. Mine is `HEAD`, or its first parent once the merge is committed and `HEAD` has two; the base is mine's parent; the update is `MERGE_HEAD`, or `HEAD`'s second parent once committed, its lineage read from its commit, one `git log`, whatever the candidate is now. A worktree with a path still unmerged, or with no merge in progress at all, as `git merge --abort` there leaves it, `theirs` then being the candidate, is reported as the update that left it reported it, the `conflict` event and the lines read from `ls-files -u`, and exit code 4 with the same message and hint; nothing changes. One with nothing unmerged and a merge in progress or committed is applied: the import branch must still point at the base, else exit code 6, `the import branch refs/heads/managed/<name> moved outside agentx while the merge was pending, so the merge cannot be applied`, with the hint `run 'agentx skill update <name> --abort' to give it up`; and the library directory must still hold mine, judged as `state` judges a directory, the fast path first, else exit code 6, `<name> was edited while its merge was pending, so the merge cannot be applied`, with the hint `run 'agentx skill update <name> --abort' and update again`. Either refusal leaves the worktree as it is, and so do those of a library entry that is a symlink or holds what git cannot record, as above. The merge, the skill's directory in the tree `write-tree` wrote, is then applied as a clean merge is, its inputs read again under the lock and one journal for the run: the import branch moves from the base to the update the merge merged, the library directory is retained and replaced with the files git ignores in it carried over, the copies that held mine or the base version are refreshed, the worktree's directory is retained and removed, and the candidate ref is deleted last, only while it still is that update, so a newer candidate a check pinned meanwhile stays as the skill's next update. git's registration of the worktree goes once the journal is applied, by `git worktree remove -f -f`; a registration an interrupted command left is pruned as below.

`agentx skill update <name> --abort` gives the merge up: under the lock, `git worktree remove -f -f <agentx home>/merges/<name>` removes the worktree and git's registration of it, whatever was resolved there. The library directory, every placement, the import branch and the candidate are left exactly as they were, and nothing is journaled. It emits the skill's `library_skill` and a `result` of `gave up the merge of <name>; the library directory is as it was`, or for a fork `gave up the merge of <name>; the fork's worktree and branch are as they were`, a fork's worktree, branch and candidate being left as they were too, printed after `✓ `. Any name with no merge pending, one the library does not hold included, is exit code 6, `<name> has no merge pending`; `--abort` does not run the judging order above.

While the worktree exists, a `skill remove` that takes the skill off the machine, with no `--from` or with `--from universal`, whether or not its library directory is still there, and a `skill update` that finds under the lock a merge that was not pending when it read the skill, are exit code 4, `<name> has a merge with its update pending, so it cannot be <updated|removed|published> until the merge is resolved or given up`, with the hint `run 'agentx skill update <name> --abort' to give the merge up; the library directory stays as it is`, and they change nothing. It is checked under the lock alone, so a refusal found before the lock comes first; a `skill publish` of a skill of a shared source, the third, is refused the same way before anything is fetched, see **Publishing a skill of a shared source**. Everything else goes on as usual: `skill place`, `--force` included, `skill remove --from <configuration>`, `skill diff` and `skill diff --update`. None of them moves the import branch, touches the worktree or replaces the library directory. `skill check-updates` goes on as usual too: it may move the candidate to a newer version meanwhile and never touches the worktree, whose `MERGE_HEAD` still names the version it merges. Every mutating command, once it has recovered the journals, removes what an interrupted command left of a pending merge: a directory under `merges/` that is no worktree git knows, its `.git` file missing or naming a git directory that is not there, and git's registration of a worktree locked with the reason `agentx pending merge` whose directory is gone, its directory under the account repo's `worktrees/`, as `git worktree prune` would remove it but for that worktree alone. It runs no git. A worktree agentx did not lock, a fork's say, is never touched. A reader that takes no exclusive lock, `skill list` and serve among them, can show such a leftover as a merge pending until the next command that changes anything removes it.

**Forks.** Your own skills, the ones `skill new` and `skill fork` make, each have a branch of their own, `refs/heads/skills/<name>`, which holds their history. agentx calls such a branch and its skill a fork, but a fork is no kind of skill: its `kind` is `managed` and its `source` the account remote, and one `skill fork` made carries the `upstream` it came from, while one `skill new` made is a fork with no upstream. A fork placed on this machine is checked out as a linked worktree at `<agentx home>/worktrees/<name>/`, locked with the reason `agentx fork` so that a prune never takes it, and the branch's tree holds the skill's directory as its one entry: so the worktree's `.git` file sits beside the skill's directory, never in it, and no client sees a `.git` entry inside a skill. That directory is named after the upstream's own directory for a fork of a managed skill, with or without a new name, after the fork for a skill `skill new` made and for a fork of an unmanaged skill or a plugin's, and after its source's for a fork of a fork; it never changes. The library entry is a symlink to it, written relative to the library directory when the library and agentx home share an ancestor other than the root, as `~/.agents/skills` and `~/.agentx` do, and absolute otherwise, both sides resolved first; a client placement points at the library entry as for any other skill, so a client reaches the fork through two symlinks, and a universal client, Codex or Gemini CLI, reads the library entry itself and gets no symlink of its own. A fork's provenance lives in the trailers of its branch's commits and nowhere else: `Agentx-Fork-ID`, a new UUID on the commit that creates the fork, which is never amended, so the id never changes, not even with the name, since a rename's commit carries none (see **Renaming a skill**); `Agentx-Machine`, the machine id, on every commit agentx writes on the branch, so a commit without it was made with git directly; and `Agentx-Base` on a merge, naming the import commit that is the fork's base after it. An import commit never carries any of the three. Its lineage is read along first parents from the tip: the first import commit is the base, the first commit with `Agentx-Base` names it, and a root that is neither means the fork has no upstream; the fork id is the nearest `Agentx-Fork-ID`, read by a walk of its own that goes on past the base. A history that does not say is reported, never guessed at from a root, a name or a common ancestor. Fork names follow the Agent Skills grammar: 1 to 64 lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, with no hyphen at the start or end and no two in a row, which every name git accepts for a branch includes and in which no two names differ only by case.

**Creating a skill.** `agentx skill new <name> [--description <text>]` creates a skill of your own: a managed skill whose source is the account remote, with no upstream. No account remote needs to be set until the skill is published: `skill new` creates the skill all the same, and `skill publish` refuses it until one is set (see **Publishing**). A name outside the grammar is exit code 6 before anything is created, `<name> is not a valid skill name: use 1 to 64 lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, with no hyphen at the start or end and no two in a row`, with the hint `choose a name such as my-skill`; `git check-ref-format --branch skills/<name>` then checks it again, which needs no repository. A description that is more than one line, holds a control character or is longer than 1024 bytes is exit code 1; without one the template says to describe the skill. The account repo is created when it does not exist yet. A branch of that name in either namespace, or one that differs from it only by case, is exit code 6, `the account repo already holds <branch name> as <one of your own skills|a skill of a shared source>[, which differs from <name> only by case]`, with the hint `choose another name`; so is anything at the library path other than a fork's symlink into `worktrees/` whose worktree is gone, `the library already holds <path>`, with the hint to choose another name or move the path aside, and anything at `worktrees/<name>`. Before the lock, the command reads the user's identity (see **Fork commits** under [Git](#git)) and writes the creation commit: no parent, a tree holding the template `SKILL.md` under `<name>/`, the Agent Skills frontmatter with the name and the description, each a plain YAML scalar when one reads back as the same string and double-quoted otherwise, so that a name such as `null` or `123` stays a string, then a heading and a line asking for the instructions, the subject `Create <name>`, and the trailers `Agentx-Fork-ID`, a new UUID, and `Agentx-Machine`. Under the lock it reads the branches, the library path and the worktree path again, sweeps the staging directories of a creation killed before its journal existed from the worktrees directory, the library and the client directories it places into, lays the commit's skill directory out in a hidden directory of the worktrees directory, never inside the worktree, and applies one journal: `ref` creating `skills/<name>` at the commit from empty, `worktree` adding the worktree, `publish` of the skill directory into it, `remove` of a dangling fork symlink at the library path when there is one, `link` of the library entry, and the placements as an install makes them, into every enabled configuration (see **Placements** above), with the settings written once when a copy is recorded. A branch of that name that appears between the read and the journal's first transaction leaves nothing changed, see [Mutation journal](#mutation-journal). The run then reads the machine again and emits one `library_skill` with `kind` `managed`, its `fork_id`, the account remote as its `source` (absent while none is set) and no `upstream`. Without `--json` it prints `✓ created <name> in <library path>: <n> placements`, then one row per placement and the universal clients, as an install does, and, while no account remote is set, the line `No account remote is set: run 'agentx source add <url> --account' before publishing <name>.`; the result summary is `created <name> in <n> configurations`.

**Forking a skill.** `agentx skill fork <name> [--name <new>]` makes a fork of a managed skill, of an unmanaged one, of a skill a plugin provides, or of one of your own skills, whose edits then have a history of their own. The fork is one of your own skills: a managed skill whose source is the account remote, and whose upstream, when it is made from a skill of a shared source or from a fork that has one, is where that skill came from; as for **Creating a skill**, no account remote needs to be set until it is published. The name is looked up in the library first, its branch saying what it is; then in the account repo, for a fork whose library entry is gone; then among the skills the plugins of every configuration provide, by the plugin's skill directory, with `<plugin>:<skill>` naming one when several plugins provide one of that name, which is otherwise exit code 6, `<skill> is provided by several plugins: <a>, <b>`. A name none of them holds is exit code 5, and a managed branch whose library directory is gone is exit code 6 with the words of its listing warning. Without `--name`, or with the skill's own name, a managed or an unmanaged skill is forked in its place: the fork takes its name and supersedes it wherever it was. With `--name <new>`, and always for a fork and for a plugin's skill, the fork is made beside the skill, which stays as it was. A fork without `--name` is exit code 6, `<name> is already a fork`, with the hint to fork it under a new name; a fork's own name is held by itself, so `--name <name>` is exit code 6, `the fork is already called <name>`, with the hint `choose another name with --name <new>`. The fork's name follows the grammar of **Creating a skill**, and a skill whose own name breaks it is refused with the hint to fork it under a valid name with `--name`; a branch of that name in either namespace, but the managed skill's own when it is forked in its place, or one that differs from it only by case, is exit code 6 with the hint `choose another name with --name <new>`. In its place, a library entry that is a symlink is exit code 6, since the move would take the link and leave the files it leads to, and so is a skill directory that holds a `.git` of its own, ignored or not, `<name> is itself a Git repository, at <path>`, with the hint to move its `.git` out of the skill or fork it beside the skill, since the move would take that repository into the worktree, where git run in the skill would find it rather than the fork's branch, and anything at `worktrees/<name>` and a library on another file system than agentx home, `the library and agentx home are on different file systems, so <name> cannot be moved into its fork's worktree`, with the hint to keep the two on one file system or fork it beside the skill; beside it, the library path and `worktrees/<new>` are refused as `skill new` refuses them. A skill whose update left a merge pending is exit code 4, `<name> has a merge with its update pending, so it cannot be forked until the merge is resolved or given up`, a fork's in the words of **Unpublished edits**; all of these come before anything is written and are asked again under the lock. The commit that records a fork's unpublished edits is then written, as **Unpublished edits** says, since the new fork starts from it: the new fork holds them too. They stay unpublished on the fork forked, while the new fork, compared with its creation commit as **Comparing a fork** says, reads `current` and `skill diff` shows nothing of them until it is edited again; its publish pushes them. A renamed skill, whose creation commit is the old one's, reads `modified` instead, see **Renaming a skill**. The fork's branch moves to that commit only once nothing else can refuse the fork, a `SKILL.md` that cannot take the new name included, just before the fork's own journal, so a refused fork records nothing. Before the lock, the command finishes any unfinished journal, reads the user's identity and writes the creation commit with the fork commit writer: the skill's directory as git records it, under every ignore rule (see **Fork commits** under [Git](#git)), so a file git ignores, a `.DS_Store` or one the skill's own `.gitignore` names, is never committed. For a managed skill the commit's one parent is its import commit, and its tree is the import's with the directory as it is now, under the upstream's own directory name: an unedited skill's creation commit has the import's tree, and an edited one's holds exactly the edits, the fork command being their explicit commit. An unmanaged skill and a plugin's skill get a parentless commit, the directory under the fork's name, the plugin's directory read and never written. A fork's new branch starts at its tip, so its history is kept, with the tip as the creation commit's parent and the tip's skill directory as its tree: an entry a commit made with git put beside the skill directory, such as a `README.md` at the worktree's root, is left out, the creation commit deleting it, since a fork's branch holds its skill directory alone. `--name` writes the new name into the frontmatter of the creation commit's `SKILL.md`: the value of the top-level `name` key is replaced, every other byte kept, a block with no such key gets it as its first line and a file with no block gets one; it is an ordinary edit with no trailer and no record of its own, and a `SKILL.md` that is not UTF-8 text or whose block is not closed is exit code 6. The subject names where the fork came from, `Fork <name> from <source URL>/<subpath> at <short upstream commit>`, `Fork <name> from plugin <plugin>` or `Fork <name> from <source name>`, and the commit carries a new `Agentx-Fork-ID` and `Agentx-Machine`, also when its tree is its parent's (the commit of `skill rename`, `Rename <old> to <new>`, carries none when the old history records one, see **Renaming a skill**), so two forks of one version, on one machine or two, have different ids and share the import commit. A Git repository nested in the directory that the ignore rules do not cover is exit code 6, as **Publishing** says; since git's ignore rules decide it, it is found once the account repo exists, so on a machine that had none, the account repo the command created for it stays, with no branch in it. Under the lock the branches, the source's branch, the library path and the source's directory are read again, and a source that changed meanwhile is exit code 6, `<name> changed while it was being forked, so nothing was changed`, with the hint to run the command again. In its place, one journal holds a `ref` step creating `skills/<name>` at the creation commit, for a managed skill `ref` steps deleting `managed/<name>` and its upstream-removed marker last, a `worktree` step adding the worktree and aligning its index with the tip, a `move` step moving the library directory, files git ignores and all, into the worktree, and a `link` step writing the library symlink where the directory was: nothing is copied, `git status` in the worktree is clean, the update candidate a check pinned stays, and every placement and `copy_mode` entry is left as it is, since they name the library path. Beside the skill, the journal is the one **Creating a skill** describes, with the commit's skill directory laid out from the account repo into the new worktree, a file the source's ignore rules keep staying with the source, and the fork placed where its source is: every configuration that has a placement of the source at its own place, a copy wherever `copy_mode` records one for the source, which it then records for the fork, and, for a plugin's skill, every enabled configuration that has the plugin and no other. A fork whose library entry is gone, forked from its branch, has no placement at its own place to follow, so the new fork is placed into no configuration; `agentx skill place` places it. The run then reads the machine again and emits the fork's `library_skill`, the source's after it when the library holds it, and for a plugin's skill one `fork_note` per configuration that has the plugin and gets the fork, placed into it or read through the library, and per universal client that does not have the plugin, since that client sees the fork through the library either way; a disabled configuration that has the plugin and does not read the library gets neither; agentx cannot turn the plugin's copy off. Without `--json` it prints a `•` line per note, then `✓ forked <name>; the fork replaces it wherever it was` in its place, or `✓ forked <name> as <new>: <n> placements; <name> stays as it was`, with `<name> from plugin <plugin>` and `the plugin's copy stays as it was` for a plugin's skill, then one row per placement and the universal clients, as an install does, and, while no account remote is set, the line **Creating a skill** names with the fork's name. The result summary is `forked <name>; the fork replaces it wherever it was` in its place, and `forked <name> as <new> in <n> configurations[, <n> placements skipped]; <name> stays as it was`, with the plugin's wording for a plugin's skill and `; always available to universal clients: <ids>` when the library is read by any, beside it.

**Publishing.** `agentx skill publish [<name>] [-m <message>]` publishes your own skills, those whose branch is a fork branch (see [Account remote](#account-remote)): it records each skill's edits, what its skill directory holds that its branch does not, as one commit on its branch, then pushes the branch to the account remote. Edits stay on this machine until they are published. Besides a publish, only a command that moves the branch records them, `skill update` and `skill fork` of the skill, and so `skill rename`, and only to keep them, unpublished (see **Unpublished edits**): agentx never records an edit unasked, serve never does, nothing does on a timer, and no commit is ever amended. A plain `git commit` in a fork's worktree is an ordinary commit of the branch that agentx never rewrites, and the next publish pushes it. With a name, the skill is judged first from what this machine holds, before the account remote is looked up or fetched: a name this machine holds neither as a branch nor as a directory of the library is exit code 5, as `skill diff` answers it, with the hint `it is one of your skills on the account remote; install it with 'agentx skill add --name <name>'` when the account remote's remote-tracking branches, as its last fetch left them, hold the name; a managed skill of a shared source is published to that source instead, see **Publishing a skill of a shared source**; and an unmanaged skill is exit code 6, `<name> is not managed, so it has no source to publish to`, with the hint `make it one of your own skills with 'agentx skill fork <name>', then publish it`, which, when the account remote's remote-tracking branches hold the name, goes on to offer installing the skill you published with `agentx skill add --name <name>` once the unmanaged directory is moved aside, since a fork made here would be another skill of the name. One of your own skills, and a bare `agentx skill publish`, which covers every one of them this machine holds and never a skill of a shared source, then need the account remote: none set is exit code 6, see [Account remote](#account-remote). A bare publish with no skill of your own prints `You have no skill of your own to publish.` with the commands that create one, and exits 0. `-m` with nothing but white space is exit code 1, `-m needs a message`, and so is one whose last paragraph holds a trailer agentx writes itself, `Agentx-Base`, `Agentx-Fork-ID` or `Agentx-Machine`, `-m may not end in a trailer agentx writes itself`, since git would read it as agentx's; both are refused before anything else, a name or the account remote included. With a bare publish, `-m` is the message of every commit the run records; when every skill of the run was judged and none has edits to record, a `warning:` says that `-m` was not used; a skill refused because its directory could not be judged may hold edits, so a run with one says nothing of `-m`.

After finishing any unfinished journal, as `skill update` does, and fetching the account remote once (see **Fetching** under [Account remote](#account-remote)), each skill is judged, and nothing is written until every skill is. Its edits first. A skill with neither a worktree nor a library entry on this machine has none. Otherwise its worktree is checked from its files: one that is gone is exit code 6, `<name>'s worktree <path> is missing`, one whose pointers no longer meet, as moving agentx home leaves them, `<name>'s worktree <path> needs repair`, both with a hint naming `agentx skill place <name>`, one where a `git merge`, `cherry-pick`, `revert`, `rebase` or `am` the user ran stopped and waits for them, whose state resetting the index would lose, `<name>'s worktree <path> is in the middle of a git <command>`, with the hint to finish it with git or run `git -C <path> <command> --abort`, one whose index a running git holds, `git is running in <name>'s worktree <path>`, with the hint to run the command again once it finishes, and one checked out on another branch `<name>'s worktree <path> is not on its branch skills/<name>`, with a hint to switch back; a skill directory missing from the worktree is exit code 6 as well. The skill directory is then captured, as the fingerprint a journal compares, and compared with the tip as `state` compares it; the tree git writes of the worktree over a throwaway index, under every ignore rule (see **Fork commits** under [Git](#git)), is the commit's: the tip's root tree with only the skill's directory replaced, so an entry a commit made with git put beside the directory stays. A Git repository nested in the directory that the ignore rules do not cover is exit code 6, `<name> holds a Git repository at <path>, which git would record as a link rather than its files`, with the hint to add it to the skill's `.gitignore` or remove it. Then the skill's branch on the account remote, as the fetch left it. A skill the remote holds no branch of, or holds an ancestor of its tip on, is pushed; one whose tip the remote holds already is pushed when it has edits, and is otherwise `up to date`; one whose remote branch is another skill is refused, see **One fork, by its id** under [Account remote](#account-remote). A publish never merges: what another machine published is taken in by `agentx skill update`, see **The account step** under [Account remote](#account-remote). So a skill the remote holds commits of that it lacks, which another machine published, is not pushed. When its tip is in the remote branch's history, as `git merge-base --is-ancestor <tip> <remote tip>` says, and it holds no edits, it has nothing to publish: it is `behind`, not a refusal, and warned about, `<name> has nothing to publish; the account remote holds changes published from another machine`, with the hint `run 'agentx skill update <name>' to take them in`, so a bare publish on a machine that has not yet taken in what another machine published still exits 0. When it also holds commits or edits of its own the remote lacks, the two diverged, as they have too when they share no commit at all: it is `moved`, exit code 6, `the account remote holds changes to <name> that this machine lacks, published from another machine`, with the hint `run 'agentx skill update <name>' to take them in, then publish again`, and its edits stay where they are, unrecorded. A merge pending on a skill is no matter of a publish's: the skill is judged by its tip as any other. Merging and retrying the push in the publish is for a later version, see [grundmanise/agentx#79](https://github.com/grundmanise/agentx/issues/79).

Then the edits of every skill that goes on are recorded, after the fetch and every refusal, so a remote git cannot reach, or one that moved on, records nothing. The files each commit changes are read in one `diff-tree` for the whole run. The subject is the first line of `-m`, the rest of it the body, or, without `-m`, `<name>: <what> (<machine label>)`, where `<what>` names the first two files by path, sorted, each with `add`, `edit` or `delete`, as in `notes: edit SKILL.md, add examples.md and 3 more files (laptop)`; every commit carries `Agentx-Machine`. Every commit is written before the lock, by the fork commit writer, with the tip as its one parent. Under the lock each skill's branch, worktree and skill directory are read again: a branch that moved, by a commit made with git meanwhile, or a directory that changed since it was captured is exit code 6 for that skill, `<name> changed while its edits were being recorded, so it was not published`, with the hint `run 'agentx skill publish <name>' again to publish it as it is now`, and nothing of it is written or pushed. One journal then holds, for each skill, a `ref` step moving `refs/heads/skills/<name>` from the tip to its commit, with the tip as its expected old value, and a `worktree` step that resets the worktree's index to the new tip, so that `git status` there is clean, every file of the directory staying as it is. A branch moved after that read, by a commit made with git between the read and the journal's first transaction, leaves nothing recorded, for any skill of the run, and is exit code 6, `<name> changed while its edits were being recorded, so nothing was published` (see [Mutation journal](#mutation-journal)).

Then every skill with something to push goes in one push, `git -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null -c push.negotiate=false -c push.pushOption= push --porcelain --no-signed --no-verify --no-recurse-submodules --no-follow-tags src-<id> refs/heads/skills/<name>:refs/heads/skills/<name> ...` in the user environment, so that a user's `push.gpgSign` or push options, which a host that takes none refuses, never apply: each refspec names the skill's own branch and none is forced, so a push carries no import branch, candidate, marker or source ref, publishing one skill leaves another skill's commits where they are, and this push never goes to a shared source, which only a publish that names one of its skills pushes to (see **Publishing a skill of a shared source**). What the remote answered for each ref is read from the porcelain status, git's stderr and its exit status, and git moves the remote-tracking branch of every ref it pushed. A ref whose status is ` `, `*` or `+` is `pushed` and `=` is `up to date`; a `Done` line, which git prints after a rejection too, or an exit status of 0 with no status for the ref, is never read as success. A ref the remote rejects because it moved on since the fetch, `fetch first`, `non-fast-forward` or `stale info`, is `moved`, with the same error and hint as a skill the publish found `moved`. A ref the host declines, `[remote rejected]` with its reason, as a hook of the remote's or a protected branch gives it, or a push the host answers with HTTP 403 that names no denial, is `declined`, exit code 6, `the account remote declined skills/<name>: <reason>`, the reason followed by up to three lines the host wrote (`remote: ...`) that name no other branch of the push, joined by `; `, with the hint `agentx never forces a push; once the account remote takes it, run 'agentx skill publish[ <name>]' again`, naming the skill the run was given and none when it was given none, and no update hint, since there is nothing to take in. A push the host denies this machine, the lines an access check reads as a denial, a credential refused or not available without asking, a host not trusted yet, or a host that wants a token or key authorised first (see **Access** under [Sources](#sources)), is `denied`, exit code 3, `the account remote <url> did not let this machine push skills/<name>: <reason>`, with a hint that fits the reason: for a host not trusted yet, to trust the host's SSH key by connecting to it once with ssh; for a host that wants a token or key authorised, to authorise it for the organisation or allow this machine's IP address; otherwise to check that the git credential helper or SSH key used for it may push there; each then to publish again as a declined push's hint says. Since no hint names a skill, a push denied for every skill of a run ends the run with that hint. A ref the remote failed to report, or one git printed no status for, is `refused`, exit code 3, `whether the account remote took skills/<name> is not known: <reason>`, with the hint to publish again. None is ever pushed again with force; the others stand. A push git could not make at all, no status and none of the answers above, is exit code 3. Edits recorded by a run whose push failed stay recorded on the branch, and the next publish pushes them: a run whose push then fails still emits the `library_skill` event of each skill whose edits it recorded, and when git could not make the push at all, exit code 3, its hint adds `the edits were recorded, so run 'agentx skill publish[ <name>]' again once the account remote can be reached`. Each skill then gets one `progress` event of phase `publish`, its `publish` event and its line, `✓ published <name> as <short commit>` or `<name> is up to date on the account remote`, or for a skill that is `behind` its warning; then, after one rescan of the machine, each skill whose edits were recorded, sorted by name, gets its `library_skill` event. The `result` is `published <name>`, `<name> is up to date on the account remote`, the warning of a skill that is `behind`, or `published <n> of <n> skills`; a run of several skills answers for those it did not publish as a run of several installs does, each named in a `warning:` line and all in the `error`, `<n> of <m> skills could not be published: ...`. Last, even when the run exits non-zero for other skills, a publish deletes the old name's branch of every skill it pushed or found up to date that was renamed on this machine (see **Renaming a skill**): a branch of the account remote, as the fetch left it, that this machine holds no skill of, whose fork id, read in one walk of every such branch, exactly one skill of the run holds, and whose name that skill's history since the branch's tip records a rename from, a commit with the subject `Rename <old> to ` on its first-parent chain, as `git log --first-parent <old tip>..<new tip>` lists it. Any other branch is left as it is, with no warning: a fork id two skills of the run hold, as on a machine that installed the renamed skill beside the old one, pairs nothing; a machine that still holds `<old>` and never renamed it leaves the renamed skill's branch be when it publishes `<old>`, since `<old>`'s history records no rename; and a branch of another fork id is never touched, so publishing a renamed skill never deletes another skill's branch. When the old branch's tip is in the renamed skill's branch there, as `git merge-base --is-ancestor` says, it is deleted, leased on that tip as **Removing a fork** deletes one, with the line `✓ deleted skills/<old> from the account remote (renamed to <new>)` and no event of its own. When it is not, another machine published to `<old>` after the rename, and it is kept, with a `warning:` `the account remote's skills/<old> holds changes <new> lacks, so it was kept` and the hint to install it beside `<new>` with `agentx skill add --name <old>` or delete it with `agentx skill remove <old> --remote`. A deletion the account remote rejects, as the default branch or a branch another machine moved since the fetch, or one git cannot make, is a `warning:` with the reason, as **Removing a fork** gives it, and the hint that the next `agentx skill publish <new>` tries again. None of these changes the exit code. A skill's branch on the account remote goes with `agentx skill remove <name> --remote`, see **Removing a fork**.

**Publishing a skill of a shared source.** `agentx skill publish <name> [-m <message>]` of a managed skill whose source is a shared source, any entry of the settings but the account remote, pushes the skill's edits to that source, to the branch the skill is installed from. It needs no account remote and never reads or fetches one, and a bare `agentx skill publish` never covers such a skill: a shared source is pushed to only when the skill is named.
