After finishing any unfinished journal, it refuses, in this order and before anything is fetched: a name the library does not hold, exit code 5; an import branch that records no version agentx can read, exit code 6; a skill whose source was removed from this machine, exit code 5, with the hint to add it again; a merge pending, exit code 4 (see **A merge pending**); a skill the last update check found its source no longer holds, exit code 6, with the hint to run `agentx skill check-updates` once the source holds it again; a skill directory holding what git cannot record, exit code 6; and a user with no `user.name` or `user.email`, exit code 6, `git has no user.name and user.email for this machine, and a publish to <url> is a commit under your own name`, with the hint to set both with `git config --global`: the machine identity a fork's commits fall back to (see **Fork commits** under [Git](#git)) never lands in a repository of others. Then the skill's edits: the library directory is compared with its base version as `state` compares it, and one that holds it has nothing to publish, `up to date` with no `commit`, the line `<name> has no edits to publish`, nothing fetched, exit code 0. Otherwise that source alone is fetched and checked as `agentx skill check-updates` checks it (see **Checking for updates**), writing the candidates and markers of its skills as a check does, while the account remote is neither fetched nor checked, so it can neither fail nor slow the publish; a fetch that fails is the publish's refusal, exit code 3. The skill's version at the fetched commit is then read as an install reads it: a folder that commit no longer holds, or a skill the check marked upstream removed, is exit code 6; a skill whose import branch moved while the check ran, by another command, is exit code 6, `<name> changed while it was being published, so nothing was published`, with the hint to publish again; a version that is what the skill directory holds now was published already, by a run that stopped after its push, and is finished as `up to date`; and one that is neither that nor the version the skill was installed at is `moved`, exit code 6, `<url> changed <name> since it was installed, so it was not published`, with the hint `run 'agentx skill update <name>' to take the change in, then publish again`, the update the check pinned for it staying where it is. A publish never merges, as for the account remote; merging and retrying is for a later version, see [grundmanise/agentx#79](https://github.com/grundmanise/agentx/issues/79). The branch is the one the skill is installed from, read with one `git ls-remote --symref src-<id> HEAD`, which with a pin also asks for `refs/tags/<pin>` and `refs/heads/<pin>`, in the user environment: the pin when the source publishes it as a branch, or with no pin the branch the remote's `HEAD` names. A pin that is a tag or a commit, or a `HEAD` that names no branch, is exit code 6, `<name> has no branch of <url> to publish to: <why>`, with the hint to add the source at the branch to publish to with `agentx source add <url>#<branch>`, update the skill from it and publish again. Then, outside the lock, the commit is written (see **Commits on a shared source** under [Git](#git)), and the import commit of the version it holds, as an install of that commit writes it (see **The import commit**), onto a staging ref of the run's own. An edit that is a symlink or a submodule link is exit code 6, `<path> is a symlink, which a publish never pushes, so <name> was not published`, and a file an install of the commit would leave out, so that the skill would not read as published, is exit code 6, `<path> cannot be published: agentx installs skills without it, so <name> was not published`. The push is `git -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null -c push.negotiate=false -c push.pushOption= push --porcelain --no-signed --no-verify --no-recurse-submodules --no-follow-tags --no-thin --force-with-lease=refs/heads/<branch>:<fetched commit> src-<id> <commit>:refs/heads/<branch>` in the user environment, with `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1`, and its answer is read as **Publishing** reads the account remote's: `pushed`, or `up to date`, finishes the publish; a branch that moved since the fetch, which the lease refuses, is `moved`, exit code 6, `<url> moved <branch> since it was fetched, so <name> was not published`, with the same update hint; a push the host declined, by a hook, a protected branch or a rule of its own, is `declined`, exit code 6, `<url> declined <branch>: <reason>`, with up to three `remote:` lines and the hint `agentx never forces a push; once <url> takes it, run 'agentx skill publish <name>' again`; a push this machine may not make is `denied`, exit code 3, `<url> did not let this machine push <branch>: <reason>`, with the hint **Publishing** gives for the reason; a push git could not make is exit code 3, `cannot reach <url>: <reason>`, with the hint to check the URL and the credentials; and a push the host answered without saying it took the branch, a `[remote failure]` status or none for the branch, is exit code 3, `whether <url> took <branch> is not known: <reason>`, with the hint `run 'agentx skill publish <name>' again; if the source took it, that finishes the publish`, since a run that finds the source holding what the skill directory holds finishes as `up to date`. Nothing is ever pushed with force, and a publish that does not go through leaves the library, the lineage and the edits as they were. Last, under the lock, one journaled mutation moves `refs/heads/managed/<name>` from the import commit the skill was installed at to the new one, and deletes the skill's update candidate when it is that same commit, as a check run after the push pins it, each ref with the value it was read at as its expected old value. The source holds the commit already, so this hold waits for a lock another command holds, for up to five seconds as a take-back does (see [Lock and version file](#lock-and-version-file)), and an interrupt does not stop it. A branch that moved meanwhile, by an update or a check, changes nothing, and the publish is reported all the same, with a `warning:` `<name> changed while it was being published, so its import branch was left as it is`, with the hint that `agentx skill check-updates` and `agentx skill update <name>` take the published version in. A lock still held after the wait, or a journal that cannot be finished, changes nothing either, and the publish is reported with a `warning:` `<url> holds what <name> published, but its import branch was not moved: <reason>`, with the hint `run 'agentx skill publish <name>' again to record it`, which finishes as `up to date`. Otherwise the skill stays managed and reads `current`. The run emits one `progress` event of phase `publish`, the `publish` event, with `branch` and `commit` once pushed, the line `✓ published <name> to <branch> of <url> as <short commit>`, or `<name> is up to date on <url>`, and, unless the skill had no edits to publish, the skill's `library_skill` event; its `result` is `published <name>`, `<name> is up to date on <url>` or `<name> has no edits to publish`, matching the line it prints. A refusal before the edits are judged emits no `publish` event; one after them carries its outcome, `moved`, `declined`, `denied` or `refused`, and a lock another command holds when the check takes it is exit code 7, as for any command, with outcome `refused`.

**Unpublished edits.** A command that moves a fork's branch, `skill update` (its account step, its update from upstream and the completion of a merge pending) and `skill fork` of a fork, and so `skill rename`, records the fork's unpublished edits first, after every refusal it can make without writing and before it judges anything against the tip: the skill directory, judged against the tip as `state` judges it, so a file git ignores never counts, becomes one commit on the branch with the tip as its parent and the subject **Publishing** generates, in a journal of its own, before the command's own. `skill fork`, and so `skill rename`, writes that commit first and moves the branch to it only once nothing else it asks can refuse, just before its own journal. The command then works on the tip that holds them, so they are never merged over or lost, and they stay unpublished: the fork reads `modified` and `skill diff` shows them until a publish pushes them. A refused command records nothing, but for a change made while it runs; a repository nested in the directory that no ignore rule covers, and, for an update, anything else git cannot record that no rule covers, which laying a new tip out would discard, are refused before the recording, exit code 6. A skill directory that changes while its edits are being recorded is exit code 6, `<name> changed while its edits were being recorded, so it was not <what the command does>`. Under the lock, after the refusal of a merge pending, exit code 4, `<name> has a merge pending, so it cannot be <what> until the merge is resolved or given up`, and before the command changes anything, a skill directory that changed since the command judged it is judged again, and one holding edits its tip does not record, made while the command ran, is exit code 6, `<name> changed while it was being <what>`, with the hint `run the command again`. Publishing, comparing and discarding the edits are not refused for them.

**History.** `agentx skill history <name>` lists the commits of a fork's branch, newest first, in one isolated `git log --no-renames --diff-merges=first-parent --root --name-status -z` of its tip: every commit reachable from it, the import commits a merge took in as its second parent included, each with the files it changes against its first parent, and a root commit against nothing, so an import lists every file it holds as added. It emits one `history` event per commit. A commit is an import when it has no parent and carries the import trailers, and foreign when it is not an import and carries no `Agentx-Machine`: it was made with git directly, by the user or an agent, and agentx keeps it as it is. Every path is relative to the skill's directory; an entry beside it, which a commit made with git may put there, starts with `../`. Nothing is written, and the fork needs no worktree on this machine: the skill directory's name comes from the library entry when that leads into the fork's worktree, and otherwise from the one directory the tip's tree holds. A skill of a shared source is exit code 6, `<name> is not a skill of the account remote, so it has no history of its own`, a directory of the library with no branch `<name> is not a skill of the account remote, so it has no history`, both with the hint to fork it, and a name neither holds is exit code 5. Without `--json` it prints one line per commit, `<short commit>  <author time to the minute>  <subject>`, then `  import` or `  foreign` for such a commit, then `  <n> files`, the subject sanitised; the result summary is `<name> has <n> commits`.

**Comparing a fork.** `agentx skill diff <fork>` shows the fork's unpublished edits, what `state` calls `modified`: how its skill directory differs from its last published version, the newest commit of its history the account remote's branch holds as the last fetch left it, read with one `merge-base <tip> <remote tip>` and nothing fetched, so edits recorded on the branch and not yet pushed stay in the diff; a renamed skill the account remote holds no branch of under its new name is compared, until it is published under it, with the branch of the nearest name its first-parent history records a rename from, a commit subject `Rename <old> to <new>` (see **Renaming a skill**), so what was published under the old name is not in the diff; a fork the account remote holds none of these branches of, or one sharing no commit with it, never published or with no account remote set, is compared with its creation commit, and one whose history carries no fork id with its tip. With `--commit <id>` it shows how the directory differs from that commit, any commit of the account repo, named by its id or anything else git reads as one, whose tree holds a directory of the skill directory's name. The commit and that directory are resolved in one `cat-file --batch-check`: a name that is no commit is exit code 6, `the account repo holds no commit <id>`, one that names several objects `<id> names more than one object of the account repo`, and a commit with no such directory `commit <short> holds no skill directory <dir> to compare <name> with`. The directory is read as `state` reads a fork's: its tree id in process first, and a directory whose id is the commit's directory costs no git beyond the lineage; otherwise git runs in the skill directory with the worktree as its work tree, over a throwaway index loaded from the commit's whole tree, then `add -A`, and `git diff --cached --relative` gives the status of every file and the diffs as it does for a managed skill, so only the skill directory is compared, every path is relative to it, and every ignore rule of the fork applies, a `.gitignore` at the worktree's root included. Nothing is written to the worktree or its index, so a worktree in the middle of a git command the user ran is read as it is; one that is missing is exit code 6, as **Publishing** says, and so is a missing skill directory. What git cannot record that no ignore rule covers is left out with a `warning:` per path and counted, as for a managed skill. The `result` is `<name> matches its last published version <short>` or `<name> differs from its last published version <short> in <n> files`, with `its creation commit <short>` or `its last commit <short>` in its place when that is what the fork was compared with, and `commit <short>` for `--commit`, and the text prints that line followed by every diff.

**Updating a fork.** `agentx skill update <fork>` takes the update the last check pinned for a fork, its candidate, by merging it, always, whether or not the fork holds commits of its own: git merges the fork's tip with the candidate, the fork's base version, the import commit its history names, given as the merge base, never one git computes or one grafted on. So the fork's own changes are kept, a line it put back to an older upstream text stays so, and what changed upstream alone comes in clean. Before it reads anything it finishes any unfinished journal, as above. A fork with a merge pending completes it instead, see **A fork's merge pending**. When the account repo has a [remote](#account-remote), the run fetches it first, once, before it selects anything, and only when it needs it: a run of one name when that name is a fork with no merge pending, `--all` when any fork is placed on this machine, one with a worktree or a library entry. The update of a managed skill never fetches it, so a remote git cannot reach neither warns nor fails it. Besides every skill with a candidate, `--all` then covers every fork placed on this machine whose branch on the account remote, as the fetch left its remote-tracking branch, read in one `for-each-ref`, is not its own tip, or whose merge is pending: the forks your other machines published to, a fork with no upstream included, need no update check first. One the account remote is behind is up to date there, with nothing said: once the account step has run, `--all` leaves out every fork with no candidate and no merge pending, a fork with no upstream included, and a run left with nothing else to do prints `Nothing to update` as above. When the fetch fails, `--all` takes nothing in from the remote-tracking branches the fetch before left, and of the forks they name selects only those with no upstream, to fail them as below, and those with a merge pending. Every fork the run covers that has no merge pending takes in first what another machine published of it, the account step (see **The account step** under [Account remote](#account-remote)), its own commit before the update's: a fork whose remote branch holds something it lacks is fast-forwarded or merged, in a journal of its own, with a `pull` event and line, and the update from upstream then merges on top of what that left, read again. A fork whose account step conflicts, and so is left pending, or is refused, exit code 6 for a different fork, emits a `pull` event of outcome `conflict` or `refused` and is not updated from upstream in that run; a fork with no upstream the account step moved has nothing more to do, and neither has a fork whose account step took its candidate away, see **The account step** under [Account remote](#account-remote): a run of one name prints that it is up to date as of the last update check, its `result` being `pulled <name> from the account remote; it is up to date with its upstream as of the last update check`, or `pulled <name> from the account remote; it has no upstream to update from` for a fork with no upstream, and `--all` goes on with the next skill. A fork with no upstream the account step did not move, since the account remote holds nothing it lacks, is up to date with the account remote: a run of one name exits 0 with `<name> is up to date with the account remote`, which is also its `result`, and emits no `pull` event, and `--all` goes on with the next skill. One the account remote holds no branch of, never published, is no failure either: a run of one name exits 0 with `<name> has no branch on the account remote; run 'agentx skill publish <name>' to publish it`, which is also its `result`, with no `pull` event. A fork with no upstream with a merge pending completes it as any fork does. A refusal of the account step names what the update could not do, as in `the account remote's skills/<name> is a different fork than <name> on this machine, so <name> cannot be updated`, and its hints name `agentx skill update <name>`. A remote git cannot reach fails every fork with no upstream the run covers with no merge pending, which has nothing else to update from, exit code 3, `cannot reach the account remote <url>: <git's reason>`, as `source add --account` says it, with a hint to check the URL and the credentials; for every other fork it is a warning, `could not fetch the account remote <url>, so <name> is updated from upstream only: <git's reason>`, naming only forks with a candidate, and the update from upstream goes on. A run that fails no fork and names none in that warning, `--all` with no fork to update from upstream or a named fork with no candidate, warns `could not fetch the account remote <url>, so what your other machines published is not taken in: <git's reason>` instead. Otherwise a fork is judged in this order: one with no upstream, with no account remote set, a skill `skill new` made or a fork of an unmanaged or a plugin's skill, is exit code 6, `<name> has no upstream to update from`; one whose history does not say which import is its base is exit code 8; one whose base came from a source with no settings entry is the refusal of a removed source above, exit code 5, which `--all` skips; one with no candidate, or with its base as its candidate, is up to date, exit 0, as above; a candidate that holds the skill under another source or subpath than the base, which no check pins, is exit code 8, `the update candidate refs/agentx/candidate/<name> holds <name> under another source or directory than its base version`, with a hint to run `skill check-updates`; a worktree git cannot work in is refused as under **Publishing**; and a skill directory holding a repository no ignore rule covers, naming it, or something else git cannot record, is exit code 6. Nothing is written before these. Then the fork's unpublished edits are recorded on its branch (see **Unpublished edits**), and the merge takes the tip that holds them, which stay unpublished. The merge is the one `merge-tree` of **Updating an edited skill**, `--merge-base=<the base> <tip> <candidate>`, with its sizing of conflict markers. A clean one is committed outside the lock by the fork commit writer (see **Fork commits** under [Git](#git)): the tree `merge-tree` wrote, the tip and the candidate as parents, the subject `<name>: merge upstream <short candidate upstream commit> (<machine label>)` and the trailers `Agentx-Base`, the candidate, and `Agentx-Machine`. Under the lock, a merge pending since is exit code 4, as under **Unpublished edits**; the branch, which must still hold the tip, else exit code 6, `<name> changed while it was being updated, so nothing was changed`, and the candidate, else the refusal of a candidate that moved above, are read again in one `for-each-ref`, the worktree's health is checked again, and the skill directory, judged again when it changed since, must hold no edits made since they were recorded. One journal then holds a `ref` step moving `refs/heads/skills/<name>` from the tip to the merge, the tip as its expected old value; the `remove` of the skill directory, retained in the worktrees directory, and the `publish` of the merge's skill directory, laid out byte for byte beside the worktree with the files git ignores in the directory carried in, but for a path the merge holds, whose file replaces the ignored one as `git checkout` replaces it, the one it replaced staying with the retained directory until the update is complete; a `worktree` step resetting the worktree's index to the branch; the copies that held the tip, refreshed as above; and the `ref` step deleting the candidate, last, when it still names the import the merge records as the fork's base. The fork reads `current` afterwards, at its new base, with no `candidate`. A merge that conflicts changes nothing: the fork's worktree, its branch and the candidate stay as they are, and the merge is left pending, see **A fork's merge pending**. `--all` updates each fork with an update in its own hold of the lock and its own journal, after the managed skills.

**A fork's merge pending.** A fork's merge that conflicts is set up as a managed skill's is, see **Conflicts**, in `<agentx home>/merges/<name>`, detached at the fork's tip, with nothing journaled: `MERGE_MSG` is the message of the commit that completes it, `<name>: merge upstream <short candidate upstream commit> (<machine label>)` with the trailers `Agentx-Base`, naming the candidate, and `Agentx-Machine`; `ORIG_HEAD` is the tip; `MERGE_HEAD` is the candidate. No ref is written for it. The command emits a `conflict` event of `kind` `managed`, prints the lines of **Conflicts**, and exits 4, `<name> conflicts with its update in <n> files, so the merge is pending and the fork's worktree and branch were left as they are`, with the hint of **Conflicts**, `<path>` being the fork's skill directory in the worktree. `skill update <fork>` run again, or `--all` while the fork still has a candidate or, with an account remote set, whenever the fork is placed here (see **Updating a fork**), reads the worktree before the lock: `git ls-files -u -z`, the files `ORIG_HEAD`, `MERGE_HEAD` and `MERGE_MSG` where `git rev-parse --git-path` says git keeps them, `git log -1 --format='%H%x00%P%x00%B' HEAD` and, when nothing is unmerged and the merge is in progress, `git write-tree`, then one `git log` walk of the commits it names. Mine is `ORIG_HEAD`; theirs is `MERGE_HEAD`, or, once the merge was committed in the worktree with `git commit`, `HEAD`'s second parent, `HEAD` being that commit. A worktree with a path still unmerged, or with no merge in progress, is reported again as above, exit code 4, and nothing changes. Otherwise the merge is completed. A branch that no longer holds the tip the merge started from is refused first, as below, so that the refusal records nothing. The fork's unpublished edits are then recorded on its branch, as above, which makes them a commit the branch gained while the merge was pending, so the completed merge is merged with them as below and they are kept. The commit that completes it is the one committed in the worktree, or one the fork commit writer writes outside the lock, the tree `write-tree` wrote with mine and theirs as parents and `MERGE_MSG` as it is as its message: the commit `git commit` would write there with that identity, with no hook, editor or clean-up of the message. A commit made in the worktree, or a `MERGE_MSG`, whose message names no base, as `git commit -m` writes it, is written again the same way with the user's subject and body and the trailers `Agentx-Base` and `Agentx-Machine` added, the base being the import theirs is, or, for theirs a completed merge or the account remote's tip, the base the rule of **The recorded base** picks of mine's base and the one that commit's history names, as the merge's own `MERGE_MSG` named it; a message that carries an agentx trailer agentx cannot read back keeps its subject alone. When the branch still holds mine, it moves to that commit, applied with the journal of a clean merge above, with the worktree's directory retained and removed after the copies, git's registration of it going once the journal is applied; the candidate is deleted only while it still names the import the commit records as the base, so a newer candidate a check pinned meanwhile stays. When the branch moved on, because commits were made in the fork's worktree while the merge was pending, `git merge-base --is-ancestor <mine> <tip>` must hold, else exit code 6, `the branch refs/heads/skills/<name> moved outside agentx while the merge of <name> was pending, so the merge cannot be applied`, with the hint to give it up with `--abort`. The completed merge is then merged with the tip on ordinary ancestry, `merge-tree --merge-base=<mine> <tip> <the completed merge>`, so that only where the new commits and the completed merge overlap can conflict. That merge records the base the completed one records, unless the commits made meanwhile brought the fork another base: then it records the newer of the two where the source history proves it, and the fork's own otherwise, by the rule of **The recorded base** under [Account remote](#account-remote); its subject is the completed merge's. A clean one is committed with the tip and the completed merge as parents and applied as above, with the tip as the branch's expected old value. One that conflicts is set up again in the same worktree under the lock, not stopped by an interrupt once started: `MERGE_HEAD` is deleted and `ORIG_HEAD` written as the tip first, so that a set-up stopped part way leaves a worktree with no merge in progress, reported again until it is given up, never one that reads as resolved; then `git update-ref --no-deref HEAD <tip>` there and the merge set up as **Conflicts** sets one up, `MERGE_HEAD`, the completed merge, last, and exits 4, `<name> conflicts with the commits made while its merge was pending in <n> files, so the merge is pending and the fork's worktree and branch were left as they are`, the `conflict` event's `base` being the tip the completed merge started from, `mine` the new tip and `theirs` the completed merge, and the line `<name> conflicts with the commits made while its merge was pending in <n> files`; the next update completes it in turn. `skill update <fork> --abort` gives any of them up as above. A merge the account step of `skill update` left pending with the account remote is the same pending merge, its `MERGE_MSG` subject `<name>: merge the account remote (<machine label>)` and its `MERGE_HEAD` the remote tip: `skill update <name>` completes it, or reports it again, by the same rules, the re-emitted line saying `<name> conflicts with the account remote in <n> files`: a merge whose `MERGE_HEAD` the remote-tracking branch holds, as one `for-each-ref --contains` says, is the account remote's, however far the remote moved on since.

**Removing a fork.** `agentx skill remove <fork>`, without `--from` or with `--from universal`, takes a fork off this machine as one journaled mutation, whether or not its worktree and its library symlink are still there: every placement by the rule every removal deletes by, then the fork's library symlink, dangling or not, or the library directory an install of the name made when its import branch is left, then the worktree `<agentx home>/worktrees/<name>/` with whatever it holds, unpublished edits and files git ignores included, retained by the journal until the mutation is complete and dropped then, then the `copy_mode` entries, and last the branch `refs/heads/skills/<name>`, deleted with the commit it was read at, an import branch of the name if one is left, its candidate ref and its upstream-removed marker. Once the journal is applied, and before the lock is released, git's registration of the worktree goes with `git worktree remove -f -f <path>`, and the branch's tracking configuration, `branch.skills/<name>`, which `skill add` with no source writes, with `git config --remove-section`, so that a later fork of the name tracks nothing; a registration a removal killed before that left is dropped, with no git, by the next mutation, once nothing names it any more: locked with the fork reason, its directory gone and its branch gone, as a loose ref and in `packed-refs` (a registration whose branch is still there is a fork whose worktree went missing, which `skill place` puts back, and is never dropped). Any other directory or file at the library path is the user's, as `skill place` and `skill list` say of it: it is left where it is, with the warning `<library path> is a directory agentx did not place there and was left as it is; <clients that read the library> still see it` (`<library path> is not <name>'s library symlink and was left as it is` for a file), counted as a placement left in place, and the line reads `✓ removed <name>: <n> placements, 1 placement left in place`. A recorded copy is judged against the library, as any removal judges one, and a copy that differs from it gets the warning `<id>'s copy of <name> was different from the library; removing it deleted those changes (<path>)` only when its skill directory also differs from every commit of the fork's branch, read in one `rev-list` and one `cat-file --batch-check`: a copy a later commit left behind holds nothing the branch lacks. A fork with a merge pending is exit code 4 before anything changes, and so under the lock, and one whose worktree's index a running git holds, a `git commit` waiting in an editor for one, is exit code 6 under the lock, `git is running in <name>'s worktree <path>`, with the hint to run the command again once it finishes or to remove the lock file named when no git is running. The branch is read again under the lock, and the journal holds it to that commit in its first transaction, before any path changes, so a branch that moved by then, a commit made with git in the worktree for one, is exit code 6, `<name> changed while it was being removed, so nothing was removed`, and leaves no journal. Without `--json` the run prints the line of any whole removal, `✓ removed <name> from the library: <n> placements`, or `✓ removed <name>, which the library no longer held: <n> placements` when its library symlink led nowhere, its rows, and `deleted <library path>, <worktree> and refs/heads/skills/<name>`, naming only what was there; the `result` is `removed <name> from the library, <n> placements, its worktree and its branch`. Nothing of the fork's account remote branch changes, and nothing on any other machine: a machine that installed the fork keeps it until it is removed there. `--remote` deletes the account remote's branch `skills/<name>` too, after the removal here, also when this machine has no fork of the name any more. agentx deletes skills only from the account remote, so a name this machine holds as a skill that is not a fork is exit code 6 before the account remote is looked up or fetched, whether or not one is set: a managed skill of a shared source, `<name> comes from <url>, and agentx deletes skills only from the account remote`, naming the source its import commit records, or `a source other than the account remote` in place of `<url>` when that commit records none git can read, and an unmanaged skill, `<name> has no source, so there is nothing to delete remotely`, both with the hint `remove it from this machine with 'agentx skill remove <name>'`. Otherwise it needs an account remote, exit code 6 without one, and fetches it first, exit code 3 when git cannot reach it, both before anything changes; a name neither this machine nor the account remote holds a skill of is exit code 5, `neither this machine nor the account remote holds a skill called <name>`, and a remote branch whose fork id is another fork's is exit code 6, `the account remote's skills/<name> is a different fork than <name> on this machine, so <name> cannot be removed from the account remote`, with the hint `remove it from this machine alone with 'agentx skill remove <name>'` (see **One fork, by its id** under [Account remote](#account-remote)). The deletion is one `git push --porcelain --no-signed --no-verify --no-recurse-submodules --no-follow-tags --force-with-lease=refs/heads/skills/<name>:<commit> src-<id> :refs/heads/skills/<name>` in the user's environment with no hook, push certificate or push option of theirs, leased on what the fetch read, so a branch another machine moved since is not deleted; git drops the remote-tracking branch with it. A push git cannot make is exit code 3, `<name> was removed from this machine, but the account remote still holds skills/<name>: <reason>`, its hint to run `agentx skill remove <name> --remote` again, which then deletes the remote branch alone. A deletion the remote rejects is exit code 3 too, `... the account remote rejected its deletion: <reason>`: a hosting service refuses to delete a repository's default branch, which a remote that only ever received fork branches has made the first of them, so the hint for `refusing to delete the current branch` is `make another branch the account remote's default branch on its hosting service, then run 'agentx skill remove <name> --remote' again`, and for a branch moved since the fetch, `stale info`, running again deletes what it holds now. The removal here prints `✓ deleted skills/<name> from the account remote` after its own lines and its `result` ends `; deleted skills/<name> from the account remote`; with no skill of the name here the `result` is `removed skills/<name> from the account remote; this machine has no skill called <name>`. `--remote` with `--from` naming a configuration is exit code 1.

**Renaming a skill.** `agentx skill rename <old> <new>` renames one of your own skills, a fork branch's, on this machine alone, and neither reads nor writes the account remote. It runs what `agentx skill fork <old> --name <new>` (see **Forking a skill**), then `agentx skill remove <old>` (see **Removing a fork**), would run, with one difference: the commit the fork step writes, subject `Rename <old> to <new>`, its parent the old tip and its tree the old skill directory with `<new>` written into SKILL.md's `name`, carries no `Agentx-Fork-ID`, so the renamed skill keeps `<old>`'s fork id (see **One fork, by its id**) and its history; when `<old>`'s history records no fork id, the commit carries a new one, so the renamed skill publishes as a fork of its own. It is placed in the configurations the old one was placed in with the same copy modes. Before either step runs, everything either would refuse is asked, so a refusal changes nothing, and `<old>`'s unpublished edits are recorded on its branch only as the fork is made (see **Forking a skill**), so the renamed skill holds them, still unpublished: until a publish, the renamed skill's `state` is `modified`, since the account remote holds no branch of `<new>` and it compares with the account remote's `skills/<old>` instead (see **Comparing a fork**), which lacks the rename commit and the carried-over edits; what was published under `<old>` is not unpublished. The refusals: a name outside the grammar or one another skill has, exit code 6, a merge pending, exit code 4, and a skill that is not one of your own, a managed skill of a shared source or an unmanaged one, exit code 6 `<old> is not a skill of the account remote, so it cannot be renamed` with the hint naming the two commands. A name another skill has is refused with the hint `choose another name with 'agentx skill rename <old> <new>'`. `<old>` and `<new>` the same is exit code 1. Each step is its own journaled mutation and recovers as that command does. The removal holds the old branch to the commit the fork was made from, and the old skill directory to the clean state the fork was judged at: one that fails once the fork is made is that failure's exit code with the message `<new> was created, but <old> could not be removed: <cause>` and the hint `run 'agentx skill remove <old>' to finish the rename`, unless that removal would delete work the new skill lacks: for edits made in the old skill since, the hint names `agentx skill remove <new>` and the rename again, which records them, to keep them, and the removal of `<old>` to drop them; for an old branch that moved since the fork, it says so and names `agentx skill remove <new>` and the rename again to keep what the branch holds now, and the removal of `<old>` to drop it. Without `--json` the run prints what each step prints, the fork's line without its `; <old> stays as it was`, then `✓ renamed <old> to <new>`; the `result` is `renamed <old> to <new>; ` followed by the removal's. The account remote's `skills/<old>` stays until the next `agentx skill publish` of `<new>`, which deletes it when it is the same skill and `<new>` holds all of it (see **Publishing**). Until then, `skill list --remote` and `skill add --all` on this machine leave `skills/<old>` out, since a skill here holds its fork id and its tip (see [Account remote](#account-remote)). Another machine keeps `<old>` installed until it is removed there, and sees `<new>`, once published, as an [installable skill](#account-remote): its tip descends from that machine's `<old>`. A publish of `<old>` there, or a bare `agentx skill publish`, puts `skills/<old>` back on the account remote, which the next publish of `<new>` on the renaming machine deletes again; install `<new>` there with `agentx skill add --name <new>` and remove `<old>` with `agentx skill remove <old>`.

**Listing.** `agentx skill list` runs a scan of the machine inside the command, reads every lineage branch in one `for-each-ref` over `refs/heads/managed/` and `refs/heads/skills/`, trailers and all, together with what the last update check left, `refs/agentx/candidate/` with the trailers of each candidate and `refs/agentx/upstream-removed/` with the commit each marker names and no message, and emits one `library_skill` event per skill directory of the library, sorted by name. A directory of the library holding no `SKILL.md` is not a skill and is not listed, whatever the account repo holds for its name: a listing reports what the clients can see, and the same rule decides what a scan reports. A directory with no branch is unmanaged: it is listed with kind `unmanaged` and no upstream, and never updated. The other way round, a managed branch whose library directory is gone, deleted or left without its `SKILL.md` outside agentx, is not listed: there is no directory to compare with its base or to place, so it has no `state` and no `drift`, and no configuration is `missing` it. It is named instead in one `warning:` per skill on stderr, after the rows, `<name> is managed in the account repo but the library holds no skill directory for it; run 'agentx skill add <url> --name <name>' to install it again, or 'agentx skill remove <name>' to stop managing it`, the URL the one its lineage records, or `<source>` for a branch that records none agentx can read, and both it and the name quoted for a shell as the warning for a kept copy quotes a name; with `--json` it is one `log` event of level `warn` with that message. A directory still at the library path, one left without its `SKILL.md`, is named in the warning instead, since an install refuses to write over it: `<name> is managed in the account repo but <path> holds no SKILL.md; move <path> aside, then run 'agentx skill add <url> --name <name>' to install it again, or run 'agentx skill remove <name>' to stop managing it`. `skill add` lays out the version the branch records only while the source still holds it: it installs the version the source holds now, and refuses with exit code `6` while the branch names another, its hint naming `skill remove`. `skill remove` clears the warning whatever the source holds, see **Removal** above. The warning costs no git process of its own, the branches being those the listing read and the library path read from the filesystem, and a [snapshot](#snapshot) carries the same warning in `warnings`. A fork that serve's reconciliation would report neither restored nor installable is named the same way, after them, read from the filesystem alone: `<name>'s worktree <path> is missing; run 'agentx skill place <name>' to check it out again from its branch`, `<name>'s worktree <path> needs repair; run 'agentx skill place <name>' to repair it`, `<name>'s library symlink <path> is missing or leads nowhere; run 'agentx skill place <name>' to put it back`, `<name>'s skill directory <path> is missing from its worktree; run 'agentx skill place <name>' to lay it out again from its branch`, the same with `, where a git <command> stopped part way` or `, where git is running` and how to finish it when the worktree is busy, `<name>'s worktree <path> holds no skill directory and is not on its branch skills/<name>; run 'git -C <path> switch skills/<name>', then run 'agentx skill place <name>'` for a worktree on another branch, and for an adopt candidate the refusal of `skill place` and its hint, joined by `; ` (see **Putting a fork back** above). Every other command that reads the lineage gives the same fork warnings once, when it first reads the branches, as `warning:` lines on stderr before its own output, or `log` events of level `warn` with `--json`, so a fork whose worktree went is named by whatever command runs next; they cost no git process of their own. `skill place` leaves out the fork it places, which it puts back instead. Leaving the library is no [drift](#serve) transition. The `state` and the `drift` of every managed skill are read in process, its directory's tree and each configuration's own place, and cost no git process of their own while the tree id tells: the tree of each import commit comes with the lineage, in the same `for-each-ref`, and so do its `candidate` and its `upstream removed`; its `pending_merge` is read with one read of the `merges` directory of agentx home. A fork's `state` and `drift` are read the same way, its tip's tree coming with the lineage too, so a fork whose skill directory holds its tip costs no git process of its own; whether its tip is published costs, with an account remote set, one `for-each-ref` of the account remote's remote-tracking branches per listing and one `merge-base` per fork whose tip differs from its remote tip, which serve keeps per fork until either of the two commits moves. A skill whose tree id does not tell costs a `read-tree`, an `add` and a `write-tree`, and a run that runs any of them one `git config` reading the user's global ignore file, see `state`. A fork's lineage costs one more git process, one `git log` over the history of every fork whose tip is not itself an import commit, whose trailers the `for-each-ref` already read (see **Forks** above); serve keeps what it read by tip, so a rescan walks only the forks whose branch moved. Lineage is read from the branches alone and never from the source refs under `refs/agentx/sources/`, so deleting every source ref between two invocations leaves the listing byte-identical, in text and in JSON. Whether a source is still added is another matter, read from the settings the listing reads anyway rather than from the account repo: a managed skill whose source has no settings entry is listed with `source removed` in `drift`, its coordinates as the lineage has them, and nothing is written for it, so removing a source changes the listing and adding it again gives back the listing from before. An account repo that does not exist is not created by a listing: every skill is then unmanaged. `skill list --remote` fetches the account remote first and lists after the rows the skills it holds that this machine has not installed, see **Installing from the account remote** under [Account remote](#account-remote); nothing else in the listing reaches the network.

**One row per configuration.** The text of `skill add`, `skill place` and `config enable --place-all` shows, for each skill, one row per configuration the command covered: the placement the rescan found at that configuration's own place, which is the library entry for a universal client and `<the client's own skills directory>/<name>` for any other. A path a client sees the skill through in another client's skills directory, as Cursor reads Claude Code's, is that other client's placement: it is that client's row when the command covered it, and never a second row of this one. A configuration the rescan finds with nothing of the skill at its own place, one whose placement `skill place` or `config enable --place-all` skipped for instance, gets one row per path it still sees the skill through instead, so that no client the command covered that sees the skill goes without a row. A copy kept for being different from the library leaves its configuration so: it does not hold the library's version, so the rescan does not find it as a placement of the skill and it has no row. The `library_skill` event is not narrowed this way: `--json` lists every path each client sees the skill through.

Without `--json`: `skill place` prints `✓ placed <name> in <n> configurations`, `<n>` counting the configurations placed into and not the rows, with `, <n> placements skipped` when there were any, then one indented row per configuration it covered, as above, `<configuration>  <mode>  <path>`, a symlink ending in ` -> <library path>`, then `  adopted <path>, ...` when any placement was adopted, then one `  discarded what <path> held` per directory `--force` discarded, then `  always available to universal clients: <ids, comma separated>` when any universal client is detected, the name sanitised and every path quoted as [Streams](#streams) prescribes, since a library directory is named by whoever made it; `skill remove` prints `✓ removed <name> from <the library|the configurations it named, comma separated>: <n> placements`, `the library` for `--from universal` too, or `✓ removed <name>, which the library no longer held: <n> placements` for a skill whose library directory was gone, with `, <n> placements left in place` when some were, then one indented row per placement it deleted, `<configuration>  <mode>  <path>`, and, for a removal without `--from`, a last indented line `deleted <library path>` followed by ` and <import branch>` when there was one, or `deleted <import branch>` alone when the library held no directory for the skill, the name and the import branch sanitised and every path quoted as `skill place` prints them; `config enable --place-all` prints `✓ <id> is now enabled`, then `✓ placed <n> skills in <id>` with `, <n> placements skipped` when some were, then one indented row per skill, as above, `<name>  <mode>  <path>`, a symlink ending in ` -> <library path>`, the name sanitised and both paths quoted as [Streams](#streams) prescribes, since a library directory is named by whoever made it; for a configuration whose client reads the library it prints `✓ <id> reads the library and already sees <n> skills; nothing was placed` in place of that second line, the rows being the library entries it sees, and its `result` is `<id> is now enabled and already sees <n> skills through the library`. `skill add` prints what `source add` prints for a source the run added or fetched, then, for each skill it installed and in that order, `✓ installed <name> from <url>[ under <subpath>] at <short upstream commit>, source fetched <last_fetched>: <n> placements`, the name and the subpath sanitised since the source supplied both, a subpath that sanitising leaves empty being quoted as a path is so that the line still names a directory, `adopted` in place of `installed` when the library already held the version, `<n>` counting the rows below it, with `, <n> placements skipped` when some were, followed by one indented row per configuration it placed into, as above, `<configuration>  <mode>  <path>`, a symlink ending in ` -> <library path>`, then `  adopted <path>, ...` when any placement was adopted, then `  always available to universal clients: <ids, comma separated>` when any universal client is detected, every path quoted; a skill the run could not install is a `warning:` line on stderr naming it and the reason, and the `error:` line at the end names them all; `skill list` prints `<n> skills` and one indented row per skill, `<name>  <kind>  <state or ->  <source>  <n placements>`, the kind being the event's `kind`, `managed` or `unmanaged`, and the source where the skill is published to: `<url>[/<subpath>]` for a skill of a shared source; for one of your own skills the account remote's URL marked as `source list` marks it, `<url> (account)`, or `account remote not set` while none is set, followed, for a forked one that has an `upstream`, by `, from <upstream>[/<upstream subpath>]`; and `(none)` for an unmanaged skill and for a managed one whose import agentx cannot read, `<n>` counting each configuration among the skill's placements once, however many paths it sees the skill through, as an install counts its rows, the state followed by `, <drift state>` for each drift state the skill is in, as in `modified, source removed`, then by `, update available` when the skill has a `candidate` and by `, merge pending` last when it has a `pending_merge`, rather than a column of its own that almost every row would leave empty, the name, and every URL and subpath in the source, sanitised as [Streams](#streams) prescribes, since a library directory is named by whoever made it and a URL or subpath by the source, or `No skills in the library. Install one with agentx skill add <source>.`

## Adoption

`agentx adopt [--all] [--skill <name>]... [--base <commit|ref>]` takes over the skills another tool installed into the library, so that agentx knows which upstream version each one is at and can update it. That tool is the vercel skills CLI, which installs into the same universal library and records what it installed in a lock file. agentx never writes that file, on any path, successful or not.

**The lock file.** It is read at `$XDG_STATE_HOME/skills/.skill-lock.json`, or at `$HOME/.local/state/skills/.skill-lock.json` when that variable is not set, and at `$HOME/.agents/.skill-lock.json`. Both are read and both may exist; the one that tool would write now – the XDG one when `XDG_STATE_HOME` is set, the `.agents` one when it is not – decides for a skill both name, and a disagreement between them is one warning naming both files. A file that is not there contributes nothing. A file that is not JSON, that holds no `skills` map, whose `skills` is not an object – `null` among them – or that is larger than 8 MiB is exit code 10 with a hint naming it, as an unreadable settings file is: a command asked to adopt what a file holds may not answer that it holds nothing. A `version` other than `3`, or none at all, is one warning and the entries are read anyway. The entries are a third party's and a user's to edit, so each is judged on its own and one bad entry never costs the others: an entry a file names twice says two things about one skill and is left out with a warning, and so is one that is not an object or that names no source, and for nothing else. A field of an entry written as something other than the text a lock file holds there is one warning naming that field, and the entry is read without it; no warning of a lock file names a type of agentx's own. `skillPath` names the skill's directory or its `SKILL.md`, written with the separator of whichever machine installed it; both are read back as one directory from the repository root, `""` at the root.

**Candidates.** One `adoption` event per entry whose library directory exists, sorted by name. An entry whose name the library cannot hold as a directory and the account repo cannot hold as a branch is left out with a warning before any path is built from it; an entry whose directory the library does not hold is no candidate at all, there being nothing on this machine to adopt. A candidate is refused, with its reason, when the library entry is not a real directory, a symlink included, when it holds no `SKILL.md`, when the name is a fork in the account repo, when the entry names a subpath that is not a directory of a repository – one that is absolute, that walks out of it, that carries a C0 control character or `DEL` or that opens or closes with whitespace, the last two being directories a repository may hold and the [import commit](#skills) cannot record on one line of a trailer – or when it names a source agentx cannot fetch: a `sourceType` other than `github`, `gitlab` or `git`, or a URL that is none of the forms `source add` accepts. A name the account repo already manages is reported as `managed` and left alone. The URL is read from the entry's `sourceUrl` first and its normalised `source` second, so that a URL agentx does not accept still leaves `owner/repo` to fall back on; a user or token in either is dropped before the URL is used anywhere and one warning says so, as it is for `source add`.

**Preview.** `agentx adopt` with no flag reports and writes nothing at all: it takes no lock, creates neither agentx home nor the account repo, makes no network call and leaves the lock file as it is. It reads the account repo's lineage branches when the repo exists, and reports a candidate whose source is not added yet with the reason that adopting adds and fetches it.

**The base version.** `--all` adopts every candidate, and `--skill <name>`, once for each, adopts the ones it names. What is recorded is the upstream version the skill was installed at, read out of the source itself; the library directory is not touched, so an edit made to it stays an edit against that version. The version is established one of three ways, tried as a cascade in the order below, each route handing on what it could not establish:

- the folder hash the entry recorded is the id of the tree the source holds at that subpath, at the source's fetched commit or at a commit behind it. Git's own content addressing is the verification, an id that names a tree naming that content and nothing else, and the search back through the history of that directory stops at 500 commits. The search follows first parents and matches the subpath literally, and it finds the newest commit of that line at which the directory holds that content, since the lock file names no commit: a version that reached the branch through a merge is found at the merge. The commit recorded is the one an install at the commit found records, the last commit reachable from it that touched the skill's directory, which holds the same version, so a merge that took the directory unchanged from one of its parents is followed to the commit that changed it. Two machines that fetched the source at two commits therefore record one commit for one version and write the same import commit for it, the one an install of that version writes. The search runs whether or not the source still holds that directory at the commit this machine fetched, an upstream-removed skill being precisely one whose version is behind the tip. A folder hash that names no version of this source (that tool's own digest of the folder, or a tree the source never held) establishes nothing and is handed on, exactly as an absent one is;
- the library directory holds exactly the version the source has at its fetched commit, which makes what is on disk an upstream version rather than an edit of one. It is the adoption an install makes for a library directory whose content hash is the version's, and the content hashes are the whole of the check: the folder hash of the lock file uses another algorithm and is comparable with nothing here. The commit recorded is the one an install at that fetched commit records, the last commit reachable from it that touched the skill's directory, which holds the same version;
- `--base <commit|ref>` names the version outright. It is exit code 1 without exactly one `--skill`, a base naming one version of one skill, and exit code 1 for a ref git would refuse. A commit id is read from the account repo, which holds the history of the source it fetched; a branch or a tag is resolved against the source itself in one `ls-remote` over the source's remote, since a fetch writes one ref per source and carries `--no-tags` and a repository's own names are therefore nowhere in the account repo. A name that is both a branch and a tag resolves to the tag and an annotated tag to the commit it peels to, which is git's own order. A name the source does not publish is exit code 5 with a hint saying what `--base` takes; one that is not in the history of the ref the source follows is exit code 6, whether it is an object another source put in the account repo or a ref of this source outside what was fetched: neither is a version of what this machine follows. The commit recorded is the last commit reachable from the one named that touched the skill's directory, which holds the same version, as an install of that commit records it. Whichever route established a version, the commit it records comes out of the reads an install makes, one history walk per source from the source's fetched commit for every skill the run adopts from it, and that walk gives the commit's committer time too. A directory no commit touched holds nothing at all, since every path a commit holds was added by one; whichever of these last two routes established its version, it refuses that skill alone with exit code 6, as a skill directory with no regular file does, and the rest of the run goes on.

**A version that cannot be established leaves the skill unmanaged.** Nothing is written for it, and the reason names every route that was tried and what each found, since the skill is left unmanaged because no route established a version and not because the first one missed. The hint names `--base`, which chooses the version, as the one explicit way on: `leave it unmanaged, or name the version it came from with 'agentx adopt --skill <name> --base <commit, branch or tag>'`. Adoption offers no diff and no fork of the directory. What the directory holds is never recorded as the version it came from, since an import commit holding an edit would be upstream content from then on and no later update could tell the two apart.

**What a run writes.** A source the settings do not hold is added exactly as [`agentx source add`](#sources) adds it, at the ref the entry recorded as its pin: the remote, the fetch, the settings entry and the `source` event, which ends the `source removed` of every skill installed from it. The first entry of that source in name order gives the pin, since a machine holds one pin per source and it governs every later install from it, and entries of one source that record different refs are one warning naming them, the one taken and how to add the source at another. A source that cannot be added costs its own skills and no others. The version's files are then read out of the account repo and the blobs this machine does not hold yet are fetched in one batch per source, as an install reads a version it is about to import, and each [content hash](#content-hash) is computed in process. The [import commit](#skills) is the commit an install of that version writes, byte for byte, and `refs/heads/managed/<name>` is created with an expected old value of empty, every branch of a run in one transaction, as one journaled mutation. That mutation has no path step at all: adoption records where a skill came from and changes nothing on disk. Every input is read again under the lock, a base that only the directory established among them, so a directory edited between the two reads is refused rather than adopted at a version it no longer holds. A branch already at this commit is the same adoption again and is left where it is; a branch at another commit, and a name taken by a fork, are exit code 6.

**Events and exit codes.** One `progress` of phase `fetch` per source the run adds and one of phase `adopt` per skill it adopts, `total` being `<skills selected> + <sources to add>`, with a skill or a source the run gives up on taking its step out of it. Then one `adoption` event per entry the run covered, in name order. `adopt` emits no `library_skill` event and runs no scan, changing no placement; [`agentx skill list`](#skills) reports an adopted skill as managed afterwards, `modified` when the directory differs from its base. A run that adopted everything it selected exits 0; a run with skills it could not adopt takes the exit code every refusal agrees on and exit code 6 when they disagree, and one skill that failed in a run that adopted nothing answers exactly as adopting that one skill would, the way an install does. `--all` with `--skill` is exit code 1. A `--skill` that names no entry of the lock file is exit code 5 listing the names it holds, and one that names an entry whose directory the library does not hold is exit code 5 saying which of the two is missing.

`adoption`, emitted by `agentx adopt`, one per entry of the lock file whose library directory exists, sorted by `name`:

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` | string | the library directory name, which is the entry's key in the lock file |
| `lock` | string | the lock file the entry was read from |
| `source` | string | the canonical URL of the source the entry names; absent when it names none agentx can use |
| `subpath` | string | the skill's directory in that source, `""` for its root; absent with `source` |
| `state` | string | `candidate` for an entry a preview could adopt, `adopted` for one this run adopted, `managed` for a name agentx already knows, `refused` for one that cannot be adopted |
| `upstream_commit` | string | the upstream commit of the base version: the last commit that touched the skill's directory, reachable from the commit the version was established at; absent when no base was established |
| `base_hash` | string | the content hash of the base version; absent with `upstream_commit` |
| `content_hash` | string | the content hash of what the library directory holds now, read again under the lock for an entry a run acted on; absent when the library no longer holds it as a directory |
| `modified` | boolean | whether the directory differs from the base version, decided as the `state` of a [`library_skill`](#event-types) is, so that `skill list` says the same afterwards; absent with `upstream_commit` |
| `reason` | string | why the entry was refused, or what an adoption would still have to do; absent when there is none |

Without `--json`: a preview prints `<n> skills of the vercel skills lock file in the library` and one indented row per entry, `<name>  <state>  <url>/<subpath> or (none)  <reason>`, the name, the subpath and the reason sanitised as [Streams](#streams) prescribes since the lock file supplied all three, followed by `Adopt them with agentx adopt --all.` when any can be. A run that adopts prints, per skill and in name order, `✓ adopted <name> from <url>[ under <subpath>] at <short commit>`, the name and the subpath sanitised as the preview sanitises them and a subpath that sanitising leaves empty quoted as `skill add` quotes one, and under it one indented `base <short content hash>: <how it was established>`, ending in `; the directory differs from it, so what differs is a local modification` when it does. A run with nothing left to do prints `Nothing left to adopt.`, and a machine whose lock file names nothing the library holds prints `No skill of the vercel skills lock file is in the library at <library>.`

## Account remote

The account remote is a Git repository the user owns, the [source](#sources) whose settings entry carries `account: true`: forks are published to it, and another machine of the user's takes them from it. It is added with `agentx source add <url> --account`, and only so. Like every source it is the remote `src-<id>` of the account repo, `<id>` the source id of its canonical URL. Only fork branches travel, both ways: `refs/heads/skills/*` and nothing else, never an import branch, an update candidate, an upstream-removed marker or a source ref, and never a tag. A machine that never sets one keeps every fork's whole history. Nothing is pushed to it, or to any source, without an explicit command.

**Setting it.** `agentx source add <url> --account` attaches the repository at `<url>`. It takes every URL form a source takes (see **URL forms** under [Sources](#sources)), and refuses, exit code 1, one with a password or a token, which it never drops for the account remote, since agentx never stores credentials, and one that names a ref or a folder, `<url> names a folder or a ref, and the account remote is a whole repository`. A plain `agentx source add <url>` of the account remote's URL adds it again as the account remote, and `--account` with another URL replaces it. The entry's `url` is the canonical URL, which forks are fetched from and pushed to (see **Pushing** under [Sources](#sources)). A shared source of the settings naming the same repository is exit code 6 before anything is read: one of the same URL, `<url> is a shared source, and a repository cannot be both a shared source and the account remote`, and one of another URL, `<url> names the repository of the shared source <shared url>, and a repository cannot be both a shared source and the account remote`, each with the hint to remove the source first with `agentx source remove <shared url>`, or to choose another repository; nothing is changed. The repository is reached first, with `git ls-remote --heads <url> refs/heads/skills/*` in the user environment, outside any repository, at the URL the remote will have; one git cannot reach is exit code 3, `cannot reach the account remote <url>: <git's reason>`, and nothing is written, not even an account repo. Then the account repo is opened, created as on its first use when it does not exist, and under the lock the remote is written into its configuration key by key, never with `git remote add`, as every source's is: `remote.src-<id>.url`, the canonical URL, `remote.src-<id>.fetch` replaced by the one refspec `+refs/heads/skills/*:refs/remotes/src-<id>/skills/*`, `remote.src-<id>.tagOpt=--no-tags`, and no `promisor` or `partialclonefilter`, which are unset, since a fork repository is never fetched without blobs. Then the remote is fetched, see **Fetching**, and what this machine may do there is checked, see **Access** under [Sources](#sources). Last, under the lock, the settings entry is written, with `last_fetched` and what the check found, and an account remote of another URL it replaces goes in the same hold: its entry, then every `branch.skills/<name>` section naming its remote is made to name the new one, then its remote and its remote-tracking branches, the branches first, then its source ref and every staging ref a killed fetch of its URL can have left. Local fork branches, worktrees and commits stay as they are, and nothing on either remote changes. A run that gets no further than its fetch, a stop included, takes the remote it wrote back, as `source add` does. The command emits the entry's `source` event, with `forks` the fork branches the fetch found, and prints `✓ the account remote is now <url>; it holds <n> forks`, followed by what the access check found, as `source add` does. `agentx source list` shows it as `<url> (account)`. `agentx source remove` of the account remote's URL or id detaches it: it removes, under the lock, every `refs/remotes/src-<id>/*`, every `branch.skills/<name>` section that names `src-<id>` and the `remote.src-<id>` section, in that order, so that a removal stopped part way leaves the remote set for the next one to take out, then its source ref and every staging ref a killed fetch of its URL can have left, then the settings entry; local branches, worktrees and commits stay as they are, and nothing on the remote changes; it emits a `source` event with the `id` and `url` alone and prints `✓ the account remote <url> is no longer set; the forks of this machine are as they were`, which is also its `result`. A password written into the configuration by hand is never printed: the URL is shown with `***` in its place.

**Fetching.** Every command that reads the account remote fetches it once, outside the lock: `git -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null fetch --quiet --no-tags --no-write-fetch-head --prune --recurse-submodules=no --refmap= [--refetch] src-<id> +refs/heads/skills/*:refs/remotes/src-<id>/skills/*` in the user environment, with `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1`, so credentials, SSH and URL rewrites apply, no hook of the user's runs, only fork branches travel and are pruned even when the user's own configuration adds a `remote.src-<id>.fetch`, a remote-tracking branch whose fork branch the remote no longer holds is deleted, and no missing object is fetched from a source along the way. The first fetch of the account remote on this machine runs with `--refetch` when an `ls-remote` before it found a fork branch (git waits for good on a refetch that has nothing to fetch), which fetches everything its fork branches reach as a fresh clone would: a shared source of the same repository, removed before or replaced by an import, may have left commits in the account repo fetched without their blobs, and a plain fetch would leave every blob those commits seem to have out of its forks' history. Which fetch is the first is read from the account repo, not the settings: one whose `remote.src-<id>` is missing, or does not have the fork refspec as its one `fetch`, was never fetched whole. That is the fetch of `source add --account`, which reads the `ls-remote` it runs before writing anything, or the first fetch after another command wrote the remote over such a one, which runs an `ls-remote` of its own. A first fetch that fails takes the remote out of the account repo again, its remote-tracking branches first, so that the next one refetches in turn. No other fetch of the account remote asks for it. Before it fetches, a command that changes something writes the remote again when it is not the one the entry wants, missing, as settings an import wrote leave it, or rewritten, under the lock, and creates the account repo when this machine has none yet; `skill list --remote` does so too. One `progress` event of phase `fetch` follows, its subject the URL. A remote git cannot reach is exit code 3 for `source add --account`, `skill publish`, `skill list --remote`, `skill add` with no source, and `skill remove --remote`, with nothing changed, for `skill update` exit code 3 for a fork with no upstream and a warning for any other fork, see **Updating a fork** under [Skills](#skills), a warning naming the source for `source fetch`, and for `skill check-updates` the failure of an unreachable source, exit code 3 once the rest is checked, see **What your other machines published** under [Skills](#skills). The entry's `last_fetched` is written by `source add --account`, `source fetch` and the update check, of `skill check-updates` and of [serve](#serve), each once its fetch succeeded; the other commands that fetch it leave it as it is. `skill publish`, `skill list --remote`, `skill add` with no source and `skill remove --remote` on a machine with no remote set are exit code 6, `no account remote is set`, with a hint naming `agentx source add <url> --account`.

**One fork, by its id.** A branch of the account remote is the same fork as the local branch of its name only when the history of each names the same fork id, the `Agentx-Fork-ID` of the nearest commit carrying one on its first-parent chain (see **Forks** under [Skills](#skills)), read with one walk of every remote tip a run covers. A remote branch whose id is another, as a skill created under the same name on two machines has, is exit code 6, `the account remote's skills/<name> is a different fork than <name> on this machine, so <name> cannot be <updated|published>`, and one where either side names no id is exit code 6 too, `the history of <name> here or of the account remote's skills/<name> records no fork id, so nothing tells that they are one fork and <name> cannot be <updated|published>`, and so is, for `agentx skill update`, a branch of the same fork whose history shares no commit with the local one, as `git merge-base` finds none, `<name> and the account remote's skills/<name> share no commit, so they cannot be merged`, which a publish reads as `moved`, see **Publishing** below: two forks of one name are never tangled, and neither is pushed over the other. The hint names the way on, `rename yours with 'agentx skill rename <name> <new>', then publish that one`. A rename keeps the fork id (see **Renaming a skill** under [Skills](#skills)), so one id can name two branches, the old name's and the new one's, until the publish of the new one deletes the old: the id compares a remote branch with the local branch of its own name, and only a publish's deletion of a renamed skill's old branch and the listing of installable skills read it across names. Nothing is ever inferred from a name, a root commit, a common ancestor or a time.

**Installing from the account remote.** `agentx skill list --remote` fetches the account remote, then lists the library as `skill list` does, then emits one `installable_skill` event per remote-tracking branch `refs/remotes/src-<id>/skills/<name>` whose name the account repo has no `skills/<name>` branch of, sorted by name, its fork id and its upstream read from the remote tip's history by the walk of **Forks** under [Skills](#skills), one `git log` for every such tip: a skill another machine published that this one has not installed. A managed skill of the name does not hide it. A branch that is a skill this machine holds under another name is left out, with no warning: one of its skills holds the branch's fork id, and that skill's tip is the branch's tip or holds it in its history, as `git merge-base --is-ancestor` says, which is the old name's branch of a skill renamed here, or on any machine that holds the renamed skill and whose history holds the old branch (see **Renaming a skill** under [Skills](#skills)); a branch of the same fork id that holds changes no such skill holds, as the renamed skill's own branch does for a machine that still holds the old name, is listed. A remote branch whose name is outside the fork name grammar is left out, with a warning naming it, since installing it could not create the local branch, and so is one whose history records no fork id, `the account remote's branch skills/<name> is left out, since its history records no fork id`, since a skill installed from it could never be updated or published. Without `--json` the rows follow the library's under the heading `<n> installable skills on the account remote`, each `<name>  managed  installable  <account remote URL> (account)[, from <upstream>[/<upstream subpath>]]`, the source written as `skill list` writes it, then `Install one with agentx skill add --name <name>, or all of them with agentx skill add --all.`; an account remote holding none prints `The account remote holds no skill this machine has not installed.` `agentx skill add` with no source installs from the account remote: `--name <name>`, once for each, the skills it names, and `--all` every one `skill list --remote` would list, less those `--except <name>` names, each matched exactly; `--to` and `--copy` place them as an install from a source does. Each is the same fork, with its fork id and its history, whose commits are published back to the same branch. With neither `--name` nor `--all` it is exit code 1, `skill add needs a source, or --name or --all to install from the account remote`, with the hint `run 'agentx skill list --remote' to see the skills the account remote holds`; `--fetch` is exit code 1, `--fetch applies to a source; the account remote is fetched every time`; `--all` with `--name`, and `--except` without `--all`, are exit code 1 as **Selecting several** says. A name given twice is installed once, and a name outside the grammar is exit code 6 before anything is read. After finishing any unfinished journal, the account remote is fetched, once for the run. With `--all`, a branch `skill list --remote` leaves out is left out with the same warning; an `--except` that names no skill the run would install is exit code 5, `the account remote holds no skill this machine lacks called "<name>"`, even when the account remote holds nothing to install; otherwise an account remote that holds nothing to install prints `The account remote holds no skill this machine lacks.` and exits 0, and an `--except` that leaves nothing to install is exit code 1, `--except left no skill to install`. Then each skill is judged and installed in turn, by name with `--all` and in the order given with `--name`, in a journal of its own. A skill that is refused costs only itself: with more than one selected, a `warning:` names it with its message, the others are installed, and the run ends with the exit code every refusal agrees on, 6 when they disagree, and an `error` naming each, `<n> of <m> skills could not be installed: <name>: <message>; ...`; one skill refused in a run that installed nothing answers exactly as installing that one skill would. For each skill: a name its remote-tracking branches do not hold is exit code 5, `the account remote holds no skill called <name>`, with the hint of a bare `skill add`; one this machine has a fork branch of is exit code 6, `<name> is already installed on this machine`, with the hint naming `agentx skill update <name>`, or, when reconciliation would call that fork installable, with neither a worktree, nor a registration of one, nor a library symlink here, `<name> is already installed on this machine, but not placed`, with the hint naming `agentx skill place <name>`; a managed skill of the name with a merge pending is exit code 4, `<name> has a merge with its update pending, so it cannot be replaced by the account remote's skill until the merge is resolved or given up`, before what the library holds is judged; a name another branch of the account repo holds whatever its case, but for the managed skill of the same name, is exit code 6 as for **Creating a skill**; a remote tip whose history records no fork id is exit code 6, `the history of the account remote's skills/<name> records no fork id, so nothing tells which skill it is and <name> cannot be installed`, since an update and a publish of it would refuse; a remote tip whose root holds no directory or several, so that nothing tells the skill directory, is exit code 8; and `<agentx home>/worktrees/<name>` existing is exit code 6, `<path> already exists, so <name> was not installed`.
